Executive summary
Stay Awhile is an explorable garden with ten real routes. Garden Entry establishes arrival and navigation. Person, Builder, Thinker, Leader, Stories, Work, Future, AURA and Contact each have a distinct spatial arrangement, camera path, content rhythm and exit. Reaching the end of a page never changes the URL. The visitor chooses every journey.
The production model is a persistent, Stay-only Three.js renderer with a small shared environmental kit and a single active destination scene. Each destination is authored in its own Blender file. HTML owns all meaningful text, project explanations, controls, evidence and navigation. Blender owns geometry and carefully bounded object motion; runtime controllers own route state, scroll sampling, responsive cameras, lighting, resource lifetime and accessible interaction.
Asset production is a prerequisite. This document does not assert that the specified Stay GLBs exist today. The audited POC is a static 60-mesh garden asset, while Quick Discovery has a separate animated world. Neither is a substitute for the ten specified Stay scenes. Production begins only after the asset gate in Part I passes, except that missing nonessential photos, links and certificate scans have explicit honest fallbacks.
The final structure requested in section 49 of the brief governs this document: thirty parts and Appendices A through L. It supersedes the earlier twenty-eight-part outline without dropping its topics. The title, contents and executive summary precede Part I as required; all detailed production specifications begin with Blender.
The source audit found ten native Stay routes, SVG and 2D canvas environments, a route-local navigation provider, an existing garden map, timer-based project demonstrations, and an AURA scrub controller. The current Quick source has fourteen stages and Blender animation; ARCHITECTURE.md and the native Stay README describe older states. Use the implementation and inspected exports as current evidence, not those stale counts.
Content boundaries are non-negotiable. MUN Club was built and run by Adharsh. STRAT MUN is a separate conference and stays outside Work. SNRLED is archived, with inactive deployment and disconnected Supabase. AI/ML is a direction, not an attained advanced engineering credential. Private relationship material and the optional appearance story do not enter public bundles. Original certificate scans and working contact values are absent from the current records.
Read this document in sequence before editing. Part I defines the production inputs; Parts II–IV define shared ownership; Parts V–XIV are route contracts; Parts XV–XXII define cross-cutting systems; Parts XXIII–XXX give files, phases, verification and handoffs. Appendices are compact lookup tables. A route implementation is complete only when its reading, motion, navigation and failure modes all satisfy the test ledger.
PART I — BLENDER / 3D ASSET PRODUCTION
1.1 Production authority and asset gate
DESIGN DECISION: author a restrained, real-time garden using low and medium complexity geometry with physically consistent scale. The scene must survive free inspection of its camera rail; it must not be a collection of flat screens behind a repeated text panel. Real 3D is justified where occlusion, object continuity, construction or spatial relations communicate an idea. Readable content remains DOM even when positioned near objects.
Part I is a production contract for the future Blender task. Sol receives the versioned exports and their validation reports; it does not create substitute runtime primitive geometry when a required model is missing. A missing required scene blocks that scene’s 3D integration gate. The readable route and defined static illustration remain available to visitors.
Asset counting: this specification defines 47 unique modeled assets, G01 through C02. A kit with multiple variants is one deliverable asset; repeated scene instances and desktop/mobile LODs do not inflate the count. Ten destination Blender masters and two protected or reserved masters are separate source files, not additional modeled assets. Verify the generated Appendix B count before handoff.
1.2 Long-term master file structure
scripts/blender/
  landing/landing-master.blend
  quick-discovery/quick-discovery-master.blend
  stay-awhile/00-garden.blend
  stay-awhile/01-person.blend
  stay-awhile/02-builder.blend
  stay-awhile/03-thinker.blend
  stay-awhile/04-leader.blend
  stay-awhile/05-stories.blend
  stay-awhile/06-work.blend
  stay-awhile/07-future.blend
  stay-awhile/08-aura.blend
  stay-awhile/09-contact.blend
The landing master is a reserved future authoring location; no landing master was found at that exact path in the audit. Do not generate or migrate it during Stay work. The existing quick-discovery-master.blend already owns Quick’s fourteen-stage world and is protected. Motion-lab blend studies under .aura-work remain archival experiments; their rendered videos are not production assets.
00-garden.blend owns the reusable kit, world anchor reference empties, arrival composition, map plan and lighting reference. The other nine files own only their route-specific geometry, layout overrides, camera guides and clips. Link shared source collections from 00-garden.blend using relative paths. Export route-specific objects separately from shared kit assets so a Sakura tree does not download again on every page.
Every source file must include a READ_ME text block containing version, purpose, asset IDs, provenance, coordinate convention, export commands, dependencies, current validation status and artist notes. No absolute machine path is required by an export script. Resolve the repository root through a passed argument and assert package.json and scripts/blender exist before writing.
1.3 Coordinate, scene and collection conventions
All coordinates in this specification are exported glTF / Three.js coordinates in metres: X right/east, Y up, Z south/toward arrival. Forward movement inside a local destination normally decreases Z. In Blender use metric units, unit scale 1, and author (xB,yB,zB)=(x,-z,y). The exporter converts Blender Z-up to glTF Y-up once. Never rotate the loaded glTF root again to “fix” up-axis. Apply object scale before export; root scale is exactly (1,1,1).
Each master uses collections 00_REFERENCE, 01_ENVIRONMENT, 02_ARCHITECTURE, 03_LANDMARKS, 04_INTERACTION, 05_ATMOSPHERE, 06_CAMERA_GUIDES, 07_LIGHT_GUIDES, 08_COLLISION_GUIDES, 09_EXPORT_DESKTOP and 10_EXPORT_MOBILE. Reference, light, collision and camera collections are non-rendering. Export selected object sets from the two EXPORT collections, including named empties. Linked common meshes remain identified by asset ID in a separate instance manifest rather than duplicated into route GLBs.
Node grammar is SA_<assetId>_<ROLE>_<two-digit-index>. Root example: SA_B02_ROOT_00. Interaction hook: SA_W02_HOTSPOT_00. Camera guide: SA_BUILDER_CAM_00. Look target: SA_BUILDER_LOOK_00. Reading clearance: SA_BUILDER_TEXT_00. Clip names in the asset records are exact, uppercase and unique within their owning asset. A static asset has clips=[], not a dummy zero-length animation.
Each route additionally exports empty nodes ENTRY, EXIT, CAM_00 through CAM_nn, LOOK_00 through LOOK_nn and TEXT_00 through TEXT_nn with the SA_<ROUTE>_ prefix. The associated <route>.scene.json records route ID, source checksum, artist version, export date, clip duration and frame count, asset IDs, instance transforms, camera knots, focus anchors, local bounds and content clearance rectangles. Geometry must leave the required camera corridor clear by 0.5 m on both sides and at least 0.25 m above the near plane.
1.4 Materials, color and lighting
Material key | Base sRGB | Roughness | Metallic | Production rule
moss | #35493A | 0.95 | 0 | Opaque vertex variation ±6%; no displacement
leaf | #5C6650 | 0.88 | 0 | Opaque solid silhouettes; mobile shares same atlas
bark | #4A423A | 0.86 | 0 | Sparse baked grain only on close timber
stone | #AAA18D | 0.92 | 0 | Desaturated warm stone, quiet roughness
sakura | #E8A6B4 | 0.78 | 0 | Sparse pale petal surfaces
paper | #F2EFE6 | 0.9 | 0 | Blank geometry; all meaningful text is HTML
water | #61796C | 0.32 | 0 | Opaque stylized reflection color; no refraction
metal | #353C38 | 0.55 | 0.25 | Only terminals and joints
clay | #8F7762 | 0.93 | 0 | Bowl without glaze highlight
accent | #B0904F | 0.58 | 0.1 | Quiet brass; not default body text
light | #EBCF9F | 0.75 | 0 | Emissive, scalar 0.35–0.8; no animated flicker
distant | #8D997B | 1 | 0 | Fogged silhouette; low contrast

Use a glTF-compatible Principled BSDF material with base color, roughness, metallic and normal inputs only. Bake procedural grain before export. Material names are SA_M_<KEY>. Colors are sRGB literals at authoring; texture color-space flags and renderer output must be verified once against a gray/ivory reference render. Keep exposure at 1.0 in the runtime baseline; do not compensate for a wrong color space by brightening all materials.
Global browser rig: one HemisphereLight with sky #F2EFE6, ground #35493A, intensity 1.4; one DirectionalLight #FFF0D6 at normalized vector (-0.45,0.8,0.3), intensity 2.0. Shadow casting is off by default; grounded appearance comes from baked vertex AO and restrained contact patches in geometry. Desktop may enable one 1024-square shadow map only after meeting performance gates. All route point lights are decorative, maximum two active, intensity ≤3, range ≤5 m; low tier uses emissive inserts only.
Route light multipliers are Garden 1.0, Person 1.05, Builder 0.92, Thinker 0.88, Leader 1.0, Stories 0.42, Work 0.82, Future 1.15, AURA 0.78→1.0 and Contact 1.02. These multiply global directional intensity; they do not change DOM text contrast. Fog is exponential with starting density 0.018, Stories 0.028 and Future 0.012. Atmosphere is runtime fog plus two subtle CSS edge washes, not volumetric meshes. No screen-wide blur or bloom pass is required.
1.5 Geometry, textures and animation export
Triangle budgets below count final triangulated geometry, not Blender polygons. Export normals and UV0 where needed; no tangents unless a normal map uses them. Meshes share materials and join static same-material pieces when doing so does not destroy object animation or occlusion. Use instances for grass, petals, small stones and repeated lanterns. Draw-call budgets are measured in the rendered browser scene, including shadow passes, not inferred from Blender object counts.
Default assets are untextured vertex/material color. The only baseline texture is a shared 1024-square timber/paper atlas with 32 px island gutters and a 512-square mobile variant. Optional route atlases must stay within Part XXI budgets and replace, not stack on top of, that base allocation. Export PNG losslessly for alpha masks and WebP for opaque baked imagery where the pipeline supports it; baseline GLBs embed PNG/JPEG only. KTX2 is an optional measured optimization after a verified fallback exists, not a production prerequisite or a reason to install a new runtime framework.
Export each asset as binary glTF 2.0: <slug>.desktop.glb and <slug>.mobile.glb in a temporary staging directory. Use selected export, mesh/material/extras/animation enabled; export no authoring lights or cameras into every prop. Rig transforms and animation are baked to transform tracks or supported morph targets at 30 fps. Apply modifiers; convert curves to mesh only in an export copy. Do not export Blender constraints, particle systems, shader graphs or Python drivers expecting them to run in Three.js.
All timeline durations are clip time, not mandatory visitor wait time. Clip time is sampled from scroll progress. For a clip with duration D and band [a,b], evaluate at D × clamp((p-a)/(b-a),0,1), including when p moves backwards. Transform keys must have a visible deterministic first and last frame. Loop clips duplicate first pose at the final frame and do not accumulate root translation. Runtime-only effects have no Blender clip. Material opacity, light intensity and visibility are in the manifest/runtime; do not assume core glTF exports arbitrary shader or light animation.
DESIGN DECISION: baseline exports need no compression decoder. Geometry simplification, material merging and mobile LOD are first-line optimizations. A later meshopt or Draco experiment must preserve node names, clip tracks and fallback support and must prove a net transfer and decode benefit. Never compress away the named interaction empties or weld independently animated objects.
1.6 Source scene contracts
00-garden.blend
Purpose and composition: A low arrival camera passes foliage and an open gate, sees the stone path, then discovers the wayfinding stone.
Collections and objects: use the collection schema in 1.3. Own G01, G02, G03, G04, G05, G06, G07, G08, G09, G10, G11, G12, G13, G14. Reference G02–G12 as needed through the instance manifest. Camera guides and look targets exactly follow Part V; lights use the route multiplier in 1.4. Desktop and mobile export collections must share anchor names and local origin.
Export and runtime boundary: 00-garden.blend produces garden.scene.json plus the 14 owned asset pairs. No typography geometry is exported. Blender owns the named meshes and clips; GardenScene.ts owns placement and clip sampling; route HTML owns narration, labels, evidence and exits. Runtime-only wind/fog/lighting remains outside Blender clips.
Animation and fallback: authored clips are exactly those listed for these owned assets; all other nodes are static. Each scene records settled poses at p=0,0.25,0.5,0.75,1. Mobile uses the low meshes and Part XIX camera transformation. Reduced motion uses the route poster plus full ordinary-flow HTML, with no animation loop. Package target for owned high assets is 1,505 KiB; low target is ≤45% of that. Scene-wide visible draw calls, textures and triangles must also pass Part XXI; individual caps are not permission to render every asset at once.

1.7 Individual asset specifications
The fields below complete the common contract in 1.3–1.5. Each record fixes purpose, shape, placement, scale, camera relationship, material, lighting, geometry, motion timing and trigger, scroll/pointer behavior, states, export names, hooks, fallback and performance. Values are authoring targets to validate in-browser, not measurements of existing assets.
G01  Terrain
Purpose and visual form: A shallow uneven garden floor with three elevation bands; paths are recessed 20 mm rather than floating above grass.
Placement and scale: (0,0,0); world kit root. Extents: 180 × 0.8 × 180 m. Positions are local to the owning route unless explicitly marked world. Ground-contact pivot at local base center; scale applied. Camera relationship: keep the silhouette visible at the owning route’s landmark beat; detail is readable from 1.8–8 m, while G09/G11 are distant silhouettes. Do not place this mesh inside the reading clearance volume.
Surface and light: moss from the material table. Use the owning scene’s light multiplier; no extra material-specific point light except G08/S02 decorative glow. Geometry cap: 18,000 desktop / 5,000 mobile triangles; target 1 material draw calls before runtime batching. High binary target ≤340 KiB, mobile ≤153 KiB, excluding the shared atlas.
Motion and trigger: Static. No displacement in browser. Scroll samples the route bands in Parts V–XIV; ambient effects use elapsed visible time and stop when hidden. Cursor behavior is none unless explicitly specified above; all meaningful interaction goes through matching HTML controls. States are unloaded, loading, ready, active, settled, failed and released. Active may seek in either direction; failed retains the poster and readable content.
Export and hooks: scripts/blender/stay-awhile/00-garden.blend → public/models/stay/v1/garden/terrain.desktop.glb (and .mobile.glb). Root SA_G01_ROOT_00; material groups SA_G01_MESH_00 onward; one SA_G01_HOTSPOT_00 only when this asset is an interactive landmark. Clip names are the uppercase action names explicitly identified as authored clips above; runtime channels and SWAY pivot names are not clips. A03 has animations=[]; static assets also have animations=[].
Fallback and performance: Use low mesh; preserve path silhouettes and horizon grade. Reduced motion shows its settled silhouette in the route poster; any explanatory sequence is reproduced as ordered HTML steps. Share geometry and material handles across instances; never clone a texture per placement. Dispose only when the asset cache reference count reaches zero. This object carries no private text, personal likeness, credential scan or invented evidence.
G02  Path Kit
Purpose and visual form: Three reusable stone path segments: straight 4 m, 30-degree bend and three-way junction; each has six irregular slab faces.
Placement and scale: Instances along graph edges and local route rails. Extents: 2.4 m clear width; 0.08 m slab thickness. Positions are local to the owning route unless explicitly marked world. Ground-contact pivot at local base center; scale applied. Camera relationship: keep the silhouette visible at the owning route’s landmark beat; detail is readable from 1.8–8 m, while G09/G11 are distant silhouettes. Surface and light: bark+accent from the material table. Use the owning scene’s light multiplier; no extra material-specific point light except G08/S02 decorative glow. Geometry cap: 3,200 desktop / 900 mobile triangles; target 2 material draw calls before runtime batching. High binary target ≤140 KiB, mobile ≤63 KiB, excluding the shared atlas.
Motion and trigger: AURA_MODEL, 3 s; shelves translate up ≤0.6 m, documents settle into sockets. Scroll samples the route bands in Parts V–XIV; ambient effects use elapsed visible time and stop when hidden. Cursor behavior is none unless explicitly specified above; all meaningful interaction goes through matching HTML controls. States are unloaded, loading, ready, active, settled, failed and released. Active may seek in either direction; failed retains the poster and readable content.
Export and hooks: scripts/blender/stay-awhile/08-aura.blend → public/models/stay/v1/aura/model-spine.desktop.glb (and .mobile.glb). Root SA_A04_ROOT_00; material groups SA_A04_MESH_00 onward; one SA_A04_HOTSPOT_00 only when this asset is an interactive landmark. Clip names are the uppercase action names explicitly identified as authored clips above; runtime channels and SWAY pivot names are not clips. A03 has animations=[]; static assets also have animations=[].
Fallback and performance: Final static pose, steps in ordinary document flow. Reduced motion shows its settled silhouette in the route poster; any explanatory sequence is reproduced as ordered HTML steps. Share geometry and material handles across instances; never clone a texture per placement. Dispose only when the asset cache reference count reaches zero. This object carries no private text, personal likeness, credential scan or invented evidence.

1.8 Complete export and validation pipeline
Production sequence: save a versioned master → build an export-only scene copy → validate units, transforms, names and bounds → bake the specified clips → export both GLBs → parse the GLB JSON and binary buffers → produce asset-manifest.json → load in the existing isolated viewer pattern → compare five canonical poses → stage under public/models/stay/v1/ only after approval of the asset gate. Do not overwrite a current valid asset with an incomplete export.
asset-manifest.json must contain schemaVersion=1, assetId, ownerRoute, relative sourceBlend path, source SHA-256, export SHA-256, byteLength, triangles, materialCount, nodeNames, clipNames/durations, bounds, desktopUrl, mobileUrl, atlas dependencies and posterUrls. Scene JSON references these IDs and transforms. The web loader rejects unsupported schema versions or missing required node names, reports a development diagnostic, and enters the route’s fallback without trapping navigation.
Example trace: 02-builder.blend → posts.desktop.glb / SA_B02_ROOT_00 / BUILD_POSTS → assets.ts entry B02 → BuilderScene.ts → evaluateBuilder(p) → mixer.setTime(1.44 × band(p,.20,.30)) → runtime render → releaseRoute(builder) → reference-counted disposal. All Appendix B rows follow this trace; no component loads a path invented outside the manifest.
Every master also produces a composed route poster from its final browser-equivalent camera: public/assets/stay/v1/<route>-desktop.webp, 1600×1000, ≤180 KiB; <route>-mobile.webp, 780×1200, ≤110 KiB. These are static fallbacks derived from the authored world, not a video replacement. Garden uses p=.55; Person .60; Builder 1; Thinker .42; Leader .48; Stories .48; Work .12; Future .85; AURA .90; Contact .50. Background image alt is empty because adjacent DOM explains the scene and its content. A meaningful standalone diagram requires its own textual description.
Asset gate: glTF validation reports no errors; all IDs and clips match; no external file references point outside public/models/stay/v1; desktop/mobile anchors differ by ≤0.01 m; camera rail has no intersection with solid geometry; first/last frames and reverse sampling match; bounding boxes stay inside the declared zone; budgets pass; screenshots show readable clearances. Check a direct mobile route without first visiting Garden. Sol must stop 3D integration of a failed asset and retain the designed fallback instead of inventing geometry.
PART II — WORLD / SPATIAL SYSTEM
2.1 One garden with distinct places
DESIGN DECISION: retain the import’s topology and landmark associations, but replace its fixed environmental backdrops with route-specific spatial rails. The garden occupies a 180 m square world. The map’s normalized plan maps into X/Z; Y remains elevation. Destinations are recognizable patches in that world, not nine copies of the same scene with another tint.
The shared floor, horizon, sunlight direction, material family, path kit and ambient phase give continuity. The active route owns its architecture, close vegetation, landmark sequence, camera rail, content anchors and local light multiplier. A distant neighbor is a low-detail silhouette only; the visitor does not download or simulate that neighbor’s complete scene. The low-detail silhouette disappears behind the map cover when the full route takes ownership of the corresponding region.
Every destination has a physical entry, interior landmark, reading zone and exit. Camera travel is constrained to its authored path. There is no free-flight camera, orbit-control navigation or joystick. Scroll moves the visitor within the active destination; explicit links move them to another route. The end pose has no timer, threshold or wheel handler that navigates elsewhere.
The visitor can open the map immediately, including before any 3D asset loads. An accessible list accompanies a real overhead plan. Location labels are descriptive, not unlock conditions. Visited status is a convenience in this session, never a score or prerequisite. Contact is always reachable in one map selection.
Place | World origin X Y Z | Local extent | Primary spatial distinction
Stay Awhile | (0,0,76) | 24 × 24 m | A low arrival camera passes foliage and an open gate, sees the stone path, then discovers the wayfinding stone.
The Person | (-42,0,40) | 18 × 24 m | An open clearing moves from a single path to a paired bench and converging roots; negative space alternates around paragraphs.
The Builder | (28,0,32) | 12 × 14 m | A workshop is constructed in eight reversible stages while the camera moves from outside the footings to the bench.
The Thinker | (-62,0,-4) | 18 × 22 m | A lateral walk beside water and a tea deck with long stationary reading poses and seven freedom stones.
The Leader | (-6,0,-2) | 18 × 18 m | Three paths meet in a courtyard; process markers coordinate around an open center rather than following a heroic central figure.
Stories | (-34,0,-40) | 12 × 22 m | Four lantern bays disclose sourced public stories; the lamps stay physically behind after the visitor passes them.
The Work | (44,0,-20) | 12 × 32 m | A longitudinal gallery contains five individual stations; camera positions alternate sides and all demonstrations explain rules.
The Future | (20,0,-70) | 20 × 30 m | The camera leaves a canopy and follows a widening path toward a distant, deliberately unreached marker.
Project AURA | (74,0,-56) | 18 × 20 m | An open garden reorganizes into an archive, evidence sheets, relationships and a revisable model spine.
Contact | (-74,0,-66) | 20 × 20 m | A nearly empty open garden with a single resting stone and quiet outward path; contacts have priority.

A direct contextual link follows the shortest graph path; it does not imply that a new physical diagonal path has appeared. Tie-break equal distances by route ordinal for deterministic travel.

Audit boundary: the checkout had existing edits in QuickDiscovery.module.css, QuickDiscovery.tsx, engine.ts and spatial-asset.ts. Those files are protected for this task and remain untouched. This specification is based on source inspection, GLB parsing, supplied/imported compositions and a fresh live study of the two external references. It is not a claim that the existing site passed current visual, accessibility or performance acceptance tests.
Repository prose has drifted: ARCHITECTURE.md still speaks of three routes and eleven Quick tracks; native/README.md still speaks of Garden-only availability. Record those as historical notes and update them only during the later authorized implementation documentation phase. The current source and supplied brief determine this specification.
2.4 Reference mechanics and transfer decisions
REF-01 — Lando Norris, https://landonorris.com/ (observed 22 September 2026). The live opening uses a portrait/helmet composition, persistent header marks and contour imagery; scrolling reaches a large typographic statement on a contrasting field. The useful principle is continuity through a recognizable anchor while scale and framing change. AURA’s adaptation is the garden path, a persistent lantern or timber spine through a transition. Exact easing, rendering technology and camera implementation were not established by visual inspection; all timings in this document are independent design decisions. Do not copy lime branding, portrait treatment, racing content or type combinations.
REF-02 — Son Daven, https://sondaven.com/en (observed 22 September 2026). The arrival frames a courtyard through architecture; an initial scroll changes apparent scale while keeping the same environment legible. Further scrolling reveals a sparse prologue with staggered word placement. The useful principles are depth framing, environmental continuity and a pause between arrival and reading. Stay uses those principles through foreground foliage, an open gate and calm reading holds. Do not reproduce the hotel imagery, ornamental display face, hotel story or its more fragmented body-word choreography. Visual inspection does not prove that its apparent camera movement is live 3D.
Quick Discovery transfer: preserve a single reversible score, overlapping preparation/arrival and semantic HTML. Change the temperament: fewer simultaneous moving objects, no camera roll, wider reading holds, capped lateral motion, organic silhouettes and restrained response to attention. Builder may feel precise; Thinker deliberately pauses; Stories reacts to approach; AURA uses the largest transformation; Contact settles. A different background color alone does not satisfy route differentiation.
PART III — ROUTING / APPLICATION ARCHITECTURE
3.1 Route contract and static export
Each URL below must return its own generated HTML on a direct request. Retain output="export", trailingSlash=true and typedRoutes. There is no dynamic catch-all that turns these routes into client-only sections. Do not require a session, Garden visit, map state or completed intro to render content.
URL | Server page | Page title
/stay/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/page.tsx | Stay Awhile — Stay Awhile
/stay/person/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/person/page.tsx | The Person — Stay Awhile
/stay/builder/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/builder/page.tsx | The Builder — Stay Awhile
/stay/thinker/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/thinker/page.tsx | The Thinker — Stay Awhile
/stay/leader/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/leader/page.tsx | The Leader — Stay Awhile
/stay/stories/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/stories/page.tsx | Stories — Stay Awhile
/stay/work/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/work/page.tsx | The Work — Stay Awhile
/stay/future/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/future/page.tsx | The Future — Stay Awhile
/stay/aura/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/aura/page.tsx | Project AURA — Stay Awhile
/stay/contact/ | src/app/(stay-awhile)/stay/contact/page.tsx | Contact — Stay Awhile

The existing route group layout retains the main landmark and EB Garamond. It gains StayExperienceProvider and a persistent StayWorldHost. Route pages export metadata and server-render their own content. A small client SceneBoundary registers routeId, DOM root and manifest ID after commit. The world host is not keyed by pathname. Route scene instances are keyed by routeId internally; the canvas is not.
Server-render content in its final readable state. Add data-stay-enhanced only after required DOM measurements and either GLB first render or a deliberate fallback decision. Scope motion-only hiding to that flag. A failed import must remove the flag and restore flow. Never send opacity:0 as the only server representation of body text. No-JavaScript users receive all route copy, real links, native details and the static garden illustration.
Keep prefetch={false} on Stay links initially because the current native code documents Windows static-export segment request issues. Do not claim that issue has been reproduced in this task. Asset prefetch is separately controlled by the manifest loader. During implementation verify client route requests against the served out/ build, not only next dev; enable framework prefetch only if measured correct and useful.
Current shared project URLs /stay/#study-planner, #past-paper-logger, #mun-club and #project-aura remain valid. The Garden page keeps the existing server-rendered legacy editorial branch for no-JS and an explicit “Open this station in The Work” link. After hydration, a known legacy project hash may replace to /stay/work/#<id> once, without animated travel or adding history. #work maps to /stay/work/; #notes to /stay/thinker/; #beyond stays on the existing legacy section; #contact maps to /stay/contact/. Unknown hashes remain in-place; never redirect arbitrary input.
3.2 State and ownership
State | Owner | Persistence / reset
routeId | Pathname observed by provider | URL is truth; reset active scene after committed pathname
routeProgress | ScrollController | Derived from measured DOM; not React state each frame
visitedIds | Stay session store | sessionStorage aura.stay.v1.visited; optional and guarded
mapOpen / focusReturn | Map controller | Ephemeral; close on route commit; restore only connected trigger
transition token / target | TransitionController | Monotonic token; invalidate on Back, new target or unmount
quality tier | QualityController | Initial capability/viewport decision; downgrade with hysteresis
motion preference | Existing preference hook + Stay toggle | OS reduce always wins; session toggle may further reduce motion
asset references | AssetStore | Shared kit + active route, optionally one pending target within cap
history scroll | Browser / Next | Do not globally force scrollRestoration manual or overwrite history.state
story/details open | Native details plus route hash | No private payloads; preserve on same-route interactions

On explicit forward navigation with no hash, start the destination at its entry pose. With a valid station/story hash, render full text and settle to that section’s readable pose. On Back/Forward, cancel travel immediately, honor browser-restored scroll, remeasure after fonts and layout, then derive visual state from that restored position. Do not replay the garden intro or reset to top on a history restoration.
Reload uses the actual pathname and hash, no module-global pendingArrival sentinel. No persisted GPU state is necessary. An optional last-scene hint may warm the cache, but must not override URL. If sessionStorage is unavailable, visited indicators simply reset. Never make navigation depend on writable storage.
Route readiness means committed DOM root, resolved measurements and either a first usable 3D frame or poster mode. It does not mean all optional images, certificate scans and nearby assets have loaded. Keep a 2.5 s visual wait budget and a 4 s route-commit watchdog. A watchdog restores visible content and offers the original real link; it does not falsely mark an unloaded route ready.
PART IV — SHARED SYSTEMS
4.1 Shared runtime division
System | Responsibility | Must not own
StayWorldHost | One renderer/canvas, sizing, WebGL context, render scheduler | Narrative copy, routing decisions or document scrolling
WorldRuntime | Scene groups, rig, per-frame evaluation, shared environment | React state updates for every animated value
AssetStore | Fetch, parse, validate, deduplicate, retain/release | Automatically load all destinations
ScrollController | Measure DOM beats; derive reversible p and reading state | Intercept native wheel/touch or turn pages automatically
CameraController | Sample route knots; apply safe responsive framing | Orbit controls, roll, inertia competing with text clock
TransitionController | Explicit travel lifecycle and cancellation | Make animation completion a navigation dependency
MotionScope | Listeners, RAF, observers and cleanup per owner | Long untracked timer chains
Content components | Semantic route text and native disclosures | Bake facts or contacts into a GLB
GardenMap | Topological plan, real links, current/visited indication | Force a sequential route order

4.2 Deterministic route score and native scroll
Use a piecewise authored score p in [0,1]. The percentages in each route table are semantic score boundaries, not a promise that every user has identical page height. Each beat owns a real HTML section with a minimum travel allocation from the route’s screen count and its score width. Measure actual section top and bottom after font readiness and resize. Map the section’s native reading interval to its authored score interval. This keeps camera/content alignment when a paragraph grows at 200% zoom.
For beat i, reading interval starts when its top reaches 70% of the viewport and ends when its bottom reaches 30%; clamp and resolve overlaps monotonically at adjacent section midpoints. Within [startY,endY], q=clamp((scrollY-startY)/(endY-startY),0,1); p=a+(b-a)q. Compute startY/endY once per measurement pass, never with layout reads inside every render frame. At document top p=0; at bottom p=1. If the page is shorter than one viewport, p follows the current visible beat and the camera uses its settled pose.
Native scrolling stays native. No Lenis, GSAP, R3F or new scroll framework is required. Imperative Three.js is already installed and fits the existing runtime pattern. Use one frame owner for camera, clip and DOM transform writes; Motion may handle discrete UI feedback only. Do not have Motion and the frame controller write the same transform.
Camera and structural clips use the same p without independent smoothing. For each beat, movement normally occupies its first 35% and holds for the remaining 65%; route-specific instructions override that ratio. Interpolate position and look target using smoothstep with zero endpoint velocity. FOV stays constant inside a route except a documented responsive change. No accumulated world-space camera offset survives a route reset.
Rapid scroll policy: if target p jumps by more than .18 between frames or implied travel exceeds 8 m/s, do not chase the missed path. Sample the destination pose and all clip end states immediately under a 120 ms low-opacity foliage wash; do not hide body copy. Text settles immediately into its correct reading state. Reverse scroll has identical deterministic evaluation, with no queued “rebuild” timers. Normal local camera travel aims below 2.5 m/s and yaw below 12 degrees/s at an ordinary one-screen-per-second gesture. These are design review targets, not scroll-speed limits imposed on the visitor.
4.3 Shared motion tokens and clock rules
Token | Duration | Use
instant | 0 / 90 ms | Focus/state changes; 0 for accessibility-critical visibility
micro | 180 ms | Button color/underline feedback
short | 320 ms | Lantern attention, disclosure ornament
medium | 620 ms | Small interface entry and light changes
long | 1100 ms | Object settling and map opening maximum
cinematic | 1800 ms | Single staged transformation reference duration
travel | 1800 ms nominal; ≤2500 ms visual wait | Inter-route itinerary, skip always available

Easing: UI entrance cubic-bezier(.16,1,.3,1); organic glide (.22,1,.36,1); UI standard (.4,0,.2,1); departure (.7,0,.84,0). Scroll interpolation uses s(t)=t²(3−2t), not a time animation repeatedly restarted by scroll. Staggers range 40–140 ms, maximum total heading stagger 240 ms. Body paragraphs use no character stagger.
Pointer offsets are optional on fine pointers only, capped at 0.06 m horizontal and 0.025 m vertical, damped with alpha=1-exp(-5dt). UI magnetism is not used in Stay. Camera roll is always zero. There is no spring on body text or camera. If a small artifact needs a settle, use a critically damped response or an authored monotonic cubic curve; overshoot cap is 2% of its travel and zero for construction joints.
Clock dt is seconds, clamped to .05 after tab restoration. Ambient time advances only while visible and unpaused. A resumed tab samples current scroll immediately, then restarts ambient time without simulating missed seconds. Hidden tab, reduced motion, context loss, static fallback and leaving the Stay route group each cancel the RAF. A Pause ambient motion control stops wind, water and petals while leaving scroll navigation and explicit disclosures usable.
4.4 Content clearances and sound
At the route’s settled reading pose, no branch, lantern or roof edge crosses the central 80% of a text line. Left text columns sit at 9% viewport width, right columns at 56–58%, centered titles at 50%; widths in the route records are maximum characters, not fixed pixels. The scene may be visible beneath a subtle local gradient, but there is no full-page repetitive ivory panel. Composition changes include open clearing, timber enclosure, water, courtyard, lantern bays, longitudinal gallery, horizon, archive and near-empty ending.
Sound decision: v1.0 is silent. No audio assets, AudioContext, autoplay controls or network requests are required. The atmosphere must work through space, light and pacing. Optional sound is a future scoped task requiring sourced/licensed audio, an explicit user enable control, independent visual progression, remembered mute preference and hidden-tab suspension. Do not leave decorative mute buttons connected to nothing.
PART V — GARDEN ENTRY
5.1 Route intent and spatial contract
Route: /stay/
Purpose: A low arrival camera passes foliage and an open gate, sees the stone path, then discovers the wayfinding stone.
Motion personality: Arrival moves from low, close foliage to an open path. A single environmental reveal establishes scale before navigation takes priority.

Lantern interaction states
State | Trigger | Visual and semantic result
DORMANT | Before bay start | Lamp .35 emission, title remains available in document
APPROACH | q ≥ .05 | Lamp .65 in 320 ms; show title in viewport
AVAILABLE | q ≥ .18 or keyboard focus | Read story summary is a native disclosure control
READING | Explicit open | STORY_SHUTTER 0→.6 s; emission .8; prose expands in normal flow
LEAVING | Section leaves viewport | Close only decorative emphasis; do not collapse a user-open disclosure
VISITED | Bay passed / opened | Lamp .45; small textual Visited status only in map if used
RETURN | Reverse scroll or anchor | Previous open state respected; no forced replay

Appearance / misperception is PRIVATE / OPTIONAL / FUTURE REVIEW; relationship/marriage content is PRIVATE. Exclude both records from public JSON, HTML, source maps and bundles. Do not show locked lanterns or provocative teasers for private stories. The four public accounts above are deliberately bounded by supplied evidence.

11.4 Route state and acceptance
States are the named labels in the environment score plus LOADING, STATIC_FALLBACK and EXIT_READY. Derive the visual state directly from p; only disclosure open/closed and explicit route travel are event state machines. Boundary ties choose the later beat except p=1, which holds the final beat. Reverse travel recomputes prior poses; all asynchronous asset completion checks the current route token before attaching.
Visual acceptance: show the entry, midpoint, final frame and every landmark hold at 1440×1000 and 390×844. The viewer must identify this route’s environment without reading its title. Confirm readable negative space, stable ground contact, no camera clipping, no unintended object pop, a stable reading hold, clear interaction affordances and an exit that feels connected to the shared garden. Text and controls must survive the identical captures with motion reduced and WebGL disabled.
Work station demonstrations and deeper content
Each station first shows its title, a 35–65 word explanation, current status and a Read the case study native details summary. Expanded details contain Problem, System, Design tradeoff, Current state and Evidence. The existing “Full case study · next pass” labels are not usable controls and must be replaced. Expansion adds ordinary document height, remeasures the route score and preserves the summary’s viewport position. Closing restores focus to the summary. No new case-study route is required in v1.0.
The case study content below is implementation-ready within the evidence supplied. A missing screenshot or repository URL becomes a clearly stated absence, not a dead View live button. No required case study needs invented metrics to appear complete. Technical details not evidenced are marked internally CONTENT GAP / REQUIRES SOURCE and omitted from public assertions.
Station | q choreography | Evidence and case-study payload
Study Planner | 0–.15 priorities; .15–.35 distribute tiles; .35–.55 move conflicting tile; .55–.70 insert break; .70–1 settled | Problem: IB workload. System: HTML/CSS/JS, local storage, decimal priorities, sessions, breaks, focus mode. Tradeoff: fair distribution over simply filling a calendar. Evidence: source description only; no verified repository/live URL.
Past Paper | 0–.20 log; .20–.40 score record; .40–.65 graph; .65–.80 dashed estimate; .80–1 hold | Problem: practice marks without a useful history. System: completed IGCSE paper logging, score records, graphs and estimates. Tradeoff: estimates are uncertain. No claim of prediction accuracy, training model or compiler functionality.
MUN Club | 0–.25 lesson; .25–.50 debate; .50–.75 simulation; .75–1 weekly structure | Problem: give younger students a structured MUN experience. System: lessons, debates, simulations and weekly operation built and taught by Adharsh. Tradeoff: coordination and attention. Real lesson plans and session photos not supplied.
SNRLED | 0–.16 register; .16–.30 payment proof; .30–.45 admin verification; .45–.65 count; .65–.82 threshold; .82–1 price tier | Problem: party registration for friends. Frontend + Supabase backend. Admin saw paid amount, people paid for and screenshot. Status archived; deployment inactive, Supabase disconnected due to low activity. Source remains on GitHub but exact URL absent.
Project AURA | 0–.25 unaligned sheets; .25–.50 evidence; .50–.70 sort; .70–.85 connect; .85–1 expression | Problem: a portfolio before adequate self-understanding. System: conversation, evidence and revision. Tradeoff: preserve uncertainty rather than flatten identity. Public process only; no download of private Codex or POS.

SNRLED logic demo: use a deterministic illustrative counter with threshold N=40, labeled “Illustrative threshold and counts — not production data”. Start verifiedPeople=38. An example party registration is for 2 people with a symbolic paid amount “Example amount”; accepting an admin verification adds 2 once, reaches 40 and changes the displayed tier from Early to Standard. Rejection adds zero. Duplicate verification adds zero. This is a visual explanation only; it does not reconstruct or claim the original backend’s concurrency logic. The real threshold, prices and exact boundary rule remain source gaps. Do not accept actual registration data or upload a payment screenshot.
Planner demo: weights .9/.7/.6/.4/.3 and twelve tiles are illustrative, never the user’s live schedule. Past Paper uses a fixed nonnumeric normalized sample series with eight points and a dashed three-point extension; screen-reader description explains the distinction. MUN panels represent process, not attendance or country allocation at STRAT MUN. AURA folders contain only public topic labels.
Station controls: Replay / Pause / Show steps are optional progressive enhancements next to the native case-study disclosure. Replay uses the same evaluator and a local 6 s maximum clock while the station is ≥50% visible; scrolling or losing focus from its control region cancels replay and samples the scroll-derived state. Never run five demonstrations continuously. Reduced motion exposes all explanatory steps immediately.

13.4 Route state and acceptance
States are the named labels in the environment score plus LOADING, STATIC_FALLBACK and EXIT_READY. Reverse travel recomputes prior poses; all asynchronous asset completion checks the current route token before attaching.

Text and controls must survive the identical captures with motion reduced and WebGL disabled.

The model graph must include a visible caption “A working model, open to revision”.

Use the world origin translation once. Roll=0. Local extent 20 × 20 m. Suggested travel allocation 1.6 viewport heights; content may extend it for zoom and translation.
