PART XV — GARDEN MAP
15.1 Plan geometry and navigation affordances
GardenMap remains a native modal dialog with an illustrated plan and an accompanying destination list. The plan must show the clearing, frame, basin, courtyard, four-lantern path, workshop, open horizon, archive and open ending at the coordinates in Part II. Use simplified SVG shapes derived from the same source scene plan; it must not be merely a list with a decorative gradient. No second WebGL canvas is needed to draw the map.
The map marker is an outlined ring for the current place, a filled small circle for visited places and a hollow small circle for other destinations. Built structures may retain squared outlines and natural places rounded outlines from the reference. Add textual Current / Visited descriptions, so color or shape is never the only distinction. All destinations are available from first load. The current route link simply closes the dialog unless its hash targets another section.
Desktop plan width is min(60vw,620px), square, with a 300px minimum list column. At widths below 900px, use a full-width plan above the list. At ≤600px height, collapse the plan to 240px and allow the dialog body to scroll; keep the Close button in a sticky dialog header. Map nodes have 44×44px hit areas at minimum, 48px on touch, even if the drawn point is smaller. If plan labels collide at 320px width, use numbered points with full text in the adjacent list; do not make text illegibly small.
Every destination control is a real anchor with its exact trailing-slash URL. The enhanced click layer only intercepts unmodified primary-button same-origin Stay links. Ctrl/Cmd/Shift/Alt-click, middle-click, download, external URLs and target=_blank retain browser behavior. The user may right-click or copy a destination link. Buttons are reserved for Open map, Close, motion controls and disclosure actions.
15.2 Open, focus and close sequence
Moment | Full motion | Keyboard / reduced-motion result
0 ms | Snapshot current route pose and invoking element | showModal(); focus Close button; background inert through native dialog
0–180 ms | Paper map surface opacity .2→1 | Map controls immediately operable; do not wait for drawing
180–620 ms | Plan scale .94→1 and perspective hint 4°→0°; current point remains anchored | Reduced: final map pose instantly, no lift
620 ms onward | Map static except requested travel trace | Tab cycles through Close, plan links, list links and optional quiet control
Escape / Close | Cancel precommit itinerary; surface exits in ≤180 ms | Close native dialog; restore connected trigger with preventScroll
Chosen destination | Transfer focus ownership to route transition | On committed route focus its h1/target after readiness; do not return to old trigger

Do not install the existing unconditional global M shortcut unchanged. Default keyboard access is the visible Map button. Optional M operates only when focus is inside the Stay navigation region, no dialog or editable control owns the key, and the shortcut can be disabled in the same controls disclosure. Escape always closes the topmost Stay modal first and never consumes browser Back. A character shortcut must not interfere with screen-reader browse commands.
Capture focus before opening. If the invoker disappears while the dialog is open, return focus to the current route’s Map button, then to its h1 if needed. Opening a certificate while the map is open is disallowed by normal focus containment; opening the map from a certificate requires closing the certificate first. Do not stack unrelated modal dialogs. Use labelled headings and aria-current=page on current-route links. Avoid announcing every intermediate point of the travel trace.
PART XVI — INTER-PAGE TRANSITIONS
16.1 One cancellable transition state machine
State | Event | Next | Action
IDLE | Valid explicit Stay link | INTENT | Create token; capture source pose; prefetch target asset budget
INTENT | Destination available | DEPART | Keep original href; mark UI busy without disabling exits
DEPART | 180 ms elapsed or skip | MAP_TRAVEL | Lift into plan; current route remains mounted under cover
MAP_TRAVEL | 420 ms or skip | COMMITTING | Call router.push once with desired URL; issue no synthetic history mutation
COMMITTING | Target pathname committed | ARRIVAL_WAIT | Register destination DOM; replace scene behind map cover
ARRIVAL_WAIT | DOM measured and 3D frame or fallback ready | ARRIVE | Focus h1/hash target; lower plan into destination
ARRIVE | 620 ms maximum | IDLE | Release source scene and cover; mark visited
Any precommit | Escape / new target / Back | CANCELLED | Invalidate token; abort target fetch; restore source pose/focus
Any postcommit | Escape / skip / timeout | IDLE | Finish at committed destination, never pretend navigation was undone

Nominal itinerary is 1800 ms: 0–180 anticipation, 180–420 plan lift, 420–1100 graph trace, 1100–1180 covered scene switch, 1180–1800 destination reveal. Commit starts at 420 ms so the network overlaps the trace. These are maximum decorative phases, not locks. A visible “Arrive now” button appears with travel and immediately completes the decorative motion. The underlying real URL remains available even if JavaScript fails.
Graph trace follows Dijkstra over the 15 edges using world distance. The marker moves by arc length along the composed route, including intermediate nodes, but intermediate Next.js routes do not mount, download or count as visited. Minimum trace time is 300 ms, nominal 680 ms and maximum 1000 ms regardless of graph length. This map-mediated journey is the selected performant approximation of continuous travel; do not pretend the camera physically flies the entire 150 m garden in one second.
The persistent canvas, camera object, light rig, shared material handles, global ambient phase and quality preference survive a Stay-to-Stay transition. The destination’s close meshes, mixers, hit anchors and local controllers are replaced. At most the source route and one small pending target bundle coexist; if residency would exceed budget, keep the source poster, release source GPU resources under map cover, and then attach the target. The map plan is sufficient spatial continuity during this exchange.
Content/scene handshake is explicit: pathname commit creates a new generation; SceneBoundary signals DOM_READY; loader signals ASSET_READY or ASSET_FAILED; renderer signals FIRST_FRAME; controller chooses REVEAL_3D or REVEAL_POSTER. All promise callbacks carry the generation ID. A late load for a canceled generation is released without attaching or changing the current loading indicator. Strict Mode remounts must not leave an old generation alive.
16.2 Interruption and history policy
New navigation during precommit replaces the target and starts a new token without stacking travel animations. After commit, a new link navigates from the committed route; decorative arrival can be skipped. Map requests during travel show the plan immediately and expose all real destination links. Escape before commit cancels; Escape after commit removes the cover and leaves the actual destination visible. Explain this through behavior, not an intrusive instructional dialog.
Back/Forward has priority over every visual phase. Cancel controller work, do not push a compensating URL, and let the router/browser restore the history entry. On the next two animation frames after stable layout, derive the route pose from restored scroll. If fonts change layout, remeasure while preserving the current reading block rather than forcing top. Refresh and new-tab entry never depend on a prior source scene.
If the target model is not usable by 2.5 s, reveal its poster and HTML and finish navigation. Asset loading may continue only if the visitor has not disabled 3D and the generation is current; replace poster with a first usable frame using 180 ms opacity, preserving camera pose. If route commit itself has not occurred after 4 s, drop the visual cover, preserve source content and show an ordinary “Open destination” link for retry. Do not schedule a hidden loop of route pushes.
Travel to /quick/ or / leaves the Stay layout and disposes all Stay resources. Use normal navigation with at most a 180 ms fade of decorative surfaces; do not implement a new landing/Quick morph. Those experiences and their current handoff behavior are protected. The browser’s link affordances and reduced-motion navigation take precedence over spectacle.
PART XVII — TYPOGRAPHY / MICRO-MOTION
17.1 Typography roles and responsive values
Role | Face | Desktop ≥1200 | Tablet 768–1199 | Mobile <768 | Line / tracking / max
Route title | Instrument Serif 400 | clamp(48px,6.7vw,104px) | clamp(42px,7vw,72px) | clamp(38px,10vw,56px) | 1.0 / -.025em / 14ch
Narrative heading | Instrument Serif 400 | clamp(34px,3.4vw,56px) | 36–48px | 30–40px | 1.12 / -.015em / 24ch
Body | EB Garamond 400 | 24px | 22px | 20px | 1.5 / 0 / 38ch
Quote | EB Garamond 400 italic | 32px | 28px | 26px | 1.35 / 0 / 28ch
Meta label | IBM Plex Mono 400 | 12px | 12px | 12px | 1.4 / .12em / natural
Nav and map label | IBM Plex Mono 400 | 12–13px | 13px | 13px | 1.4 / .06em / wrapping
Evidence detail | Inter 400 | 16px | 16px | 16px | 1.5 / 0 / 55ch
Status | IBM Plex Mono 500 | 12px | 12px | 12px | 1.5 / .04em / natural

Use existing next/font variables; do not request new external runtime fonts. --font-display-face is Instrument Serif, --font-meta-face IBM Plex Mono, --stay-body-face EB Garamond and --font-body-face Inter. Scope Stay’s role aliases in stay-world.module.css. Desktop body is not reduced to fit a frame. Avoid uppercase long prose; uppercase is for short route ordinals and utility labels only.
Primary ink is #23291F on a sufficiently quiet light reading field, or #F2EFE6 against a controlled dark field in Stories. Secondary light-field ink is #4A5144. Sakura and brass are accents, never the only cue or default paragraph color. Measure final composited contrast at the brightest and darkest scene frame behind every text region; add a local solid/gradient reading wash where needed. Main body contrast target ≥4.5:1, large text ≥3:1, focus boundaries ≥3:1. Text-shadow alone is not a contrast guarantee.
Layout role changes do not change the underlying content. One h1 per page; h2 for narrative beats and project/stories; h3 inside details. Decorative split words are aria-hidden duplicates only if there is a single equivalent semantic heading that remains discoverable. Prefer inline word spans within the real heading with spaces preserved and no duplicated screen-reader text. Never split long body paragraphs into per-character spans.
17.2 Text animation contract for every block
ID | Coverage | Initial → settled | Timing
TH | Every route h1 and major beat heading | Whole words, bottom mask; 18px y, opacity 0→1; 40ms word stagger capped at 240ms | q .06–.20 reveal; .20–.28 settle; .28–.92 hold; no visible text exit
TS | Subheading / supporting sentence | Whole line, clip from left 100→0%; x=8→0px; opacity .2→1 | q .12–.24; settle .24–.28; hold to section exit
TP | Every body paragraph | Whole paragraph; y=8→0px; opacity .2→1; no blur or letter staggering | q .16–.28; siblings offset .02q, final visible by .34; hold
TL | Ordinal, source, archive/status label | Whole label; left mask; tracking .16→.12em; opacity .3→1 | q .08–.18; hold with related heading
TN | Navigation and exit labels | No entry hiding for persistent nav; exit links opacity .4→1, y=4→0px | Exit beat q .10–.20; hover/focus 180ms underline only
TM | All map labels | Immediate readable text; optional plan-opacity share only | No word staggering; visited/current updates instant
TE | Evidence and certificate copy | Immediate readable text on open, opacity optional .7→1 | ≤180ms, no transform needed
TQ | Major reflective quotation | Whole phrase grouping, y=8→0px, bottom mask | q .06–.18; camera already slowing; hold ≥60% of beat

The complete route block inventory is the beat list in Parts V–XIV: <route>-bNN. Apply TH to its Heading, TP to each Public copy paragraph and TL to any explicit ordinal. The route’s first heading is h1; subsequent headings are h2, with one semantic h2 for multiple displayed AURA process labels. Work station titles use TH, their status uses TL, case-study subheads use TS and detail bodies use TP only on initial disclosure; then they remain visible. Story disclosure labels use TN and story paragraphs use TP. Person certificate titles use TE. Contact channel labels use TN with no hiding, values use TE. Every exit link uses TN and every map label TM.
The fixed font-size and tracking values above are the reading state. Do not animate font-size or line-height: those cause layout shifts and interfere with the measured scroll score. Tracking animation is limited to short labels and a 0.04em change. Heading blur, if used at all, is ≤2px during the first 15% of its reveal and always zero by settling; the baseline specification uses zero blur. No blurry paragraph is allowed.
Entry precedes reading, never withholds it. If focus enters a block, a hash target is loaded, scroll jumps past a reveal band, or reduced motion is enabled, evaluate its final readable state in the same frame. Body text is never dimmed to .22 or .34 after being read as the current reference controllers sometimes do. Link focus must not disappear because the parent’s animation crossed an opacity threshold.
Typography composition changes with the environment: Person alternates the clear side of the path; Builder captions align with staged joints; Thinker has longer quiet measures beside water; Leader names concrete responsibilities; Stories rests beside a lamp; Work couples explanation to the active station; Future uses larger, shorter phrases with more sky; AURA uses four complete narrative sentences then process labels; Contact remains nearly still.
PART XVIII — INTERACTION DESIGN
18.1 Interaction contract ledger
ID / target | Trigger | Response / duration | Reset | Reduced
I01 Map control | Click / Enter / Space | Open native dialog; plan lift ≤620ms | Escape closes; focus returns | Instant map
I02 Destination link | Unmodified click / Enter | Explicit route itinerary ≤1800ms | New target cancels precommit; Back wins | Normal navigation
I03 Landmark attention | Fine pointer within 140px / focus of matching DOM control | Tint or emissive +.15, 180–320ms standard ease | Leave resets in 320ms | Color/outline only
I04 Story lantern | Native details open / close | Shutter 0↔18°, 600ms; emission .8 | No auto-collapse on scroll | Instant open prose
I05 Work case study | Native details summary | Expand content in document flow; ornamental 180ms fade | Closing preserves focus and remeasures | Instant height
I06 Work replay | Replay button | Local deterministic 4–6s sequence, one at a time | Scroll, route exit or visibility loss cancels | Show numbered steps
I07 Certificate | Expand button | Dialog with image, caption and Close; 180ms opacity | Escape returns focus to exact trigger | Instant dialog
I08 Water | Explicit optional Ripple button, never intercept page drag | One 2.4s ring, amplitude .01m; max one | Ends naturally; new activation replaces | Static highlighted ring
I09 Foreground foliage | Fine pointer approach | ±.03m displacement, 620ms organic glide | Leave decays; no active global hit testing on touch | Static
I10 Petals | Pointer proximity on fine pointer only | Local drift offset ≤.02m, 320ms | Return to seeded trajectory; no accumulation | No petals
I11 Motion controls | Click / Enter / Space | Pause ambient or choose Read without motion | Preference change cancels ongoing decorative work | OS reduce dominates
I12 Contact channels | Click/Enter on validated link | Native mailto/tel/HTTPS | No success toast claiming delivery | Unchanged
I13 Onward path | Click/Enter | Same route-travel controller as map | No hover-triggered route change | Normal link
I14 Same-route station anchor | Click/Enter | Native anchor position, short optional 320ms scroll | Wheel/touch/keys cancel optional scroll | Instant anchor
I15 Skip link | Keyboard activation | Focus main/route h1 and preserve visibility | No animation needed | Unchanged

The cursor remains the browser cursor. Do not recreate the landing custom cursor in Stay. Hover means preview only; content and navigation never depend on it. Every actionable 3D landmark has a visible matching DOM link or button. Decorative meshes are aria-hidden and pointer-events:none at the canvas layer; they do not become inaccessible canvas-only controls.
PointerController reads at most one pointer event sample per animation frame and acts only on the active landmark. Do not raycast the whole garden every frame. DOM hit targets determine intent; raycasting is optional for decorative local feedback and disabled on coarse pointers. Drag is unnecessary in v1.0. Do not hijack touch scrolling, pinch zoom or swipe-back. No water gesture or artifact dragging may prevent ordinary page movement.
Interaction hierarchy is route navigation first, readable content second, meaningful demonstrations third and decorative environmental response last. Disable the last category first on weak hardware. Feedback must not shift target positions: no moving button away from a pointer, magnetism, full-scene shake or uncontrolled spring. Avoid cursor-dependent camera movement during a reading hold on Thinker and Contact.
18.2 Certificate system
Implement a Stay-scoped CertificateDialog using the existing achievement records and the same Expand / Close language as Quick’s CertificateArchive. Do not import QuickDiscovery.module.css or edit its component. Trigger includes achievement title and “View certificate”. The current asset alt text and a visible caption must say placeholder / original scan pending. Grade 5 Music Theory, Grade 5 Guitar Practical and ISSO Nationals Shot Put Bronze Medalist use identical behavior.
Dialog uses a heading, image with meaningful alt, visible evidence status and Close button. Native showModal provides focus containment; explicitly record and restore the trigger. Escape and Close both work. Fit the image inside max-width:min(90vw,900px), max-height:70dvh with object-fit:contain; offer an ordinary original-image link only when a real file exists. Do not label the placeholder as a verified scanned certificate. Image failure leaves title, status and close control visible.
No certificate year, issuing body, score, guitar distinction, shot-put distance or age category is supplied by the current records. Do not add these. There is no photo proof for MUN Club or House activity in the audited public assets; display a short evidence-pending sentence in a disclosure rather than a large fake photograph slot in the main composition.
PART XIX — RESPONSIVE / MOBILE
19.1 Responsive system
Mode | Condition | Geometry/render | Camera and text
Wide | ≥1200px, height >600 | DPR ≤1.5; high meshes; two-column compositions | FOV 46°; authored x rail; copy max 38ch
Medium | 768–1199px, height >600 | DPR ≤1.25; medium density, low foliage geometry | FOV 50°; x rail ×.65; body 22px
Narrow | <768px or coarse pointer | DPR ≤1.25; mobile assets; one text column | FOV 52°; x rail ×.35; add 1m camera setback
Short | height ≤600px | Reading-first flow, no pinned body regions | Visual band ≤40vh; controls not fixed over text
Low power | Performance downgrade / save-data | DPR 1; no point lights, water waves or wind | Static scene on demand, or poster if still slow
Reduced | OS reduce or Read without motion | No WebGL requirement; all text flow | Route poster + complete semantics

Use viewport width for layout, pointer capabilities for hover and measured frame health for quality. A phone is not automatically forced into a blank static page; capable phones receive the authored low-detail world. Conversely, a wide integrated-GPU laptop may need the low-power tier. No device brand sniffing. Save-data disables speculative asset prefetch and defaults to poster until the visitor selects 3D.
Mobile composition is separately authored through the Part V–XIV rules, not a CSS scale() applied to a 1440px scene. Put explanatory charts in responsive SVG with readable labels, not tiny textures. The Work aisle is centered and stations alternate above the reading column. Stories retains four bays but shallower bends. AURA reduces orbit-like lateral motion and uses frontal relationships. Thinker and Contact maximize stationary views.
Use min-height based on svh for travel allocation, and dvh only for viewport-fitting dialogs where browser chrome changes matter. ResizeObserver records the world host rectangle. Do not restart all route animation on every mobile address-bar pixel change; coalesce measurements and preserve the active content block. Orientation change remeasures once stable, then samples that block’s current score.
Safe areas: controls inset by max(16px, env(safe-area-inset-*)); reserve at least 64px above first route copy for navigation. Bottom location plaque is hidden on short screens if it overlaps prose; the current route name remains in nav/map. At 320px CSS width there must be no horizontal document overflow. Map and certificate dialogs may scroll vertically. Touch targets are 48px; gaps between adjacent targets are ≥8px.
19.2 Per-route responsive acceptance matrix
Route | Mobile design requirement | Static/reduced replacement
Stay Awhile | Use a centered rail, camera height 1.2→1.55 m, fronds confined to outer 12% of the viewport and title width ≤11ch. The map control is visible from first paint. | garden poster at p=0.55; all 6 beats in document flow
The Person | Camera lateral x is multiplied by .35; use one readable column at 20 px horizontal padding. Bench and roots frame the upper visual region, never the body copy. | person poster at p=0.6; all 8 beats in document flow
The Builder | Move camera to (0,1.7,z+1.5), reduce side travel to 35%; bench occupies top 40%, copy flows below. Preserve all five project labels without shrinking. | builder poster at p=1; all 8 beats in document flow
The Thinker | Hold camera at (1.2,1.6,z+1), remove pointer offsets and waves in low tier. Body uses 20–22 px Garamond; freedom labels are a wrapping semantic list, never tiny stone textures. | thinker poster at p=0.42; all 7 beats in document flow
The Leader | Use centered camera and shallow convergence; process labels stay in DOM. At 320 px wrap the five responsibilities vertically and preserve their order. | leader poster at p=0.48; all 6 beats in document flow
Stories | Reduce side bends by 40%; every lantern has a 48 px HTML Read story button below its title. The story text stays in normal flow and does not require hover. | stories poster at p=0.48; all 6 beats in document flow
The Work | Narrow camera aisle at x=0 and FOV 52; station props occupy top 36% while HTML flows underneath. Use explicit Previous/Next station anchor links, no horizontal swipe requirement. | work poster at p=0.12; all 7 beats in document flow
The Future | Preserve a central horizon at 42% viewport height, no roll. Text starts below the sky opening; direction labels wrap at 320 px. Remove far foliage first. | future poster at p=0.85; all 5 beats in document flow
Project AURA | Use a frontal 52-degree camera, movement depth scaled .6 and no orbit. Nine documents retain full labels in a separate ordered list. On low tier use five stage illustrations and the same prose. | aura poster at p=0.9; all 8 beats in document flow
Contact | One column, 24 px gaps, 48 px targets. No camera travel after entry, no floating contact text, no hover-only actions. | contact poster at p=0.5; all 3 beats in document flow

PART XX — ACCESSIBILITY / REDUCED MOTION
20.1 Semantic and keyboard rules
Retain the root SkipLink and the existing main landmark in the Stay layout. Do not nest another main in every page. Each route has one h1 and meaningful section headings; the DOM reading order matches the narrative order even where desktop text alternates left and right. The canvas is aria-hidden because an equivalent explanation exists in HTML. Never put essential content exclusively in WebGL, a background image or a tooltip.
Focus order follows visual task order: home, map, motion controls, route introduction, relevant disclosures/links and onward paths. Noninteractive visual objects are not tabbable. On direct route entry do not steal focus; on explicit client navigation focus the h1 or valid hash target with preventScroll after its final location is established. On Back, respect restored focus when the browser provides it; otherwise focus the current route heading without resetting scroll.
Map and certificate dialogs must meet the WAI-ARIA modal interaction pattern: contained focus, a visible close action, Escape dismissal and sensible focus return. The specification’s use of native dialog is an implementation choice. Source: W3C APG Dialog Modal Pattern, https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/dialog-modal/ . Test actual browser behavior rather than assuming a library guarantees it.
Focus rings use a 2px outline with 3px offset and remain visible above gradient washes. In forced colors, use system Highlight and remove decorative backgrounds where they obscure boundaries. Do not suppress outlines without a replacement. Reading mode preserves links, status captions and all evidence labels. Screen readers must not receive duplicate decorative headings, petal labels or 60-times-per-second live region updates.
20.2 Motion reduction and ambient pause
The OS prefers-reduced-motion setting wins immediately, including when changed while a route is open. Stop ambient RAF, cancel travel decoration, settle the active scene, remove sticky travel-only heights and reveal all route text. If WebGL was already allocated, release it after the poster is visible; do not leave a hidden renderer running. If the preference becomes full motion, enhancement is optional and must preserve the current reading section; do not replay the arrival.
Read without motion is an additional user control for vestibular comfort or low power. It does not override an OS request to reduce. Pause ambient motion separately stops auto-moving petals, branches and water that would otherwise continue longer than five seconds. Scroll-linked movement continues only in full mode; keyboard scrolling remains native. Essential progress does not require decorative movement.
Every route’s fallback is defined in its chapter, and the full content exists in the initial HTML. Stories uses native details; Work uses ordered system steps; Builder shows the completed frame with the construction steps in text; AURA presents its four narrative sentences and five process stages sequentially. Do not merely shorten durations to 1ms while retaining a broken pinned composition.
20.3 Readability and alternative access
Concern | Required behavior | Verification
Contrast | 4.5:1 body, 3:1 large text and key UI edges | Measure composited worst-case frames; no text-only shadow workaround
Zoom/reflow | 200% text zoom and 400% browser zoom retain access | 320px-equivalent viewport; no two-direction reading scroll
Images | Useful alt for evidence; empty alt for decorative poster | Image failure and screen-reader reading pass
Map | Plan plus complete real-link list | Keyboard-only any destination in one selection
3D failure | Complete HTML and scene poster | Disable WebGL and block GLB requests
Disclosure | Native summary and in-flow details | No-JS and Escape where dialog applies
Announcements | One concise route-arrival status if needed | No continuous camera/scroll announcements
No traps | All routes retain map/home/exit links | Tab, Shift+Tab, Escape, Back and touch swipe-back

PART XXI — PERFORMANCE / WEBGL
21.1 Performance budgets and measurement conditions
Metric | Desktop target | Mobile target | Measurement
First route HTML/CSS/Stay JS | ≤250 KiB gzip incremental over shared app | ≤220 KiB gzip | Measure transfer; do not count browser cache as zero-cost first visit
Shared garden kit first use | ≤1.8 MiB compressed transfer | ≤700 KiB | Only visible shared assets; G01 world floor may use low tier initially
Active route unique GLBs | ≤2.4 MiB | ≤900 KiB | Work may approach cap; individual asset caps are upper bounds
Initial visible poster | ≤180 KiB | ≤110 KiB | Responsive source, not both downloaded
Total first route 3D transfer | ≤4.5 MiB including kit and route | ≤1.8 MiB | No other destination bundle before intent
Visible triangles | ≤160,000 | ≤55,000 | Count instances and any shadow pass
Visible draw calls | ≤90 baseline / 110 with optional shadow | ≤45 | Renderer.info.render.calls at worst frame
Texture GPU allocation | ≤64 MiB estimated | ≤24 MiB estimated | Include mipmaps, render targets and duplicate uploads
Total GPU residency estimate | ≤128 MiB | ≤64 MiB | Estimate buffers and textures; GPU memory is not directly portable
Active animated transform nodes | ≤80 | ≤30 | Static objects do not need animation tracks
Particles | 48 garden; 8 thinker; 0 contact | 8 garden; 2 thinker; 0 contact | Instanced; drop first on downgrade
Frame time after warm-up | p95 ≤20 ms at 1440×900 | p95 ≤33 ms at 390×844 | 60-second trace on named hardware, foreground, no devtools recording overlap
Layout shift | CLS ≤0.1 | CLS ≤0.1 | Fonts/images sized; case-study click expansion excluded only as expected input shift
Interaction latency | INP target ≤200 ms | INP target ≤200 ms | Field metric requires traffic; use lab interaction traces as proxy
Main-thread stalls | No animation-caused task >50 ms repeatedly | Same | Decode/parse may need chunking and poster-first reveal

These are proposed budgets, not measured current results. Record test device, browser, viewport, DPR, network profile, warm/cold cache and frame count with every performance report. Target content LCP ≤2.5 s on an ordinary mobile connection and a simulated 4 Mbps / 150 ms cold-cache profile; the poster and semantic text must render before GLB completion. A 3D transfer budget that cannot complete in 2.5 s does not justify delaying the content. Benchmark with the production static export.
Load priority: immediately load route HTML/CSS/fonts and its correctly sized poster; after hydration and full-motion capability, dynamically import the Stay runtime and fetch shared assets actually needed by the route. On route entry fetch required unique scene assets. On approach within one beat, load the next Work station if not already in the route bundle; when a user focuses or hovers a destination for 150 ms, prefetch at most one target manifest and its essential low-detail assets. On demand load original certificate images and optional evidence. Never prefetch the whole garden on idle.
Implement progressive Work loading through six independent W assets, not a monolithic file. First entry needs W01 shell and W02 station. Retain W03–W06 only when approached or selected through a hash. A direct #snrled entry loads shell plus W05 first; it must not wait for Planner, Logger and MUN. Keep the case-study HTML available regardless of station asset residency.
21.2 Quality tiers and stable adaptation
High tier uses desktop meshes, DPR capped 1.5, two decorative point lights and normal particle density. Medium uses low foliage and desktop landmarks, DPR 1.25, one point light, half particles. Low uses mobile meshes, DPR 1, no point lights or optional shadows, static foliage and opaque water. Poster mode allocates no WebGL. Select the initial tier conservatively from viewport/pointer/save-data; do not rely on navigator.deviceMemory being present.
Sample frame health in 2 s windows after 3 s warm-up. If p95 exceeds 28 ms on desktop or 40 ms on narrow view for three consecutive windows, downgrade one tier at the next reading hold or under map cover. Do not change silhouettes mid-motion. Upgrade only by explicit user choice in v1.0; this avoids oscillation. If low tier still exceeds 50 ms p95 for three windows, offer “Use the quiet version” and switch to poster if the user chose an automatic performance mode. Do not hide or remove content to hit a benchmark.
Renderer uses alpha=false where the stage can own its background, antialias=true only if measured affordable, outputColorSpace=SRGBColorSpace and ACESFilmicToneMapping with exposure 1. Create one renderer in StayWorldHost. Resize its drawing buffer only when rounded device-pixel dimensions change. Render when scroll, pointer, UI or asset state changes; continue RAF only while visible ambient motion is active. In paused mode schedule a single frame per change.
21.3 Resource cleanup contract
AssetStore keys by canonical versioned URL and variant. Fetch with AbortController, then parse the ArrayBuffer. Abort cancels fetch; parsing may not be cancellable, so late parse resolution must check generation and release its new resources. A loader failure cannot leave stale status or attach an old scene. Deduplicate simultaneous requests and reference-count shared geometries, materials and textures. Do not dispose a shared tree material when one route unmounts.
Route release order: invalidate generation → detach DOM interaction listeners and observers → cancel route RAF/timers/replay → stop all mixer actions → uncache mixer root → remove route scene group → decrement asset references → dispose zero-reference geometry, materials and textures → close unshared ImageBitmap images → clear maps of node/DOM references. A route controller’s dispose method is idempotent. Do not call renderer.dispose on every route switch; the renderer belongs to the Stay layout.
Stay layout release additionally aborts pending loads, releases the shared kit, disposes renderer and any render targets, removes canvas, unregisters context and resize listeners and clears the scheduler. No global anonymous listener or interval may survive. Context loss prevents new renders and reveals poster/HTML immediately; one controlled retry may recreate the renderer on explicit request. Do not loop automatic forceContextLoss/restore attempts.
Memory verification: traverse Garden→Work→AURA→Contact→Garden twenty times. After two warm-up cycles, loaded GPU geometry/texture counts and owned listener/RAF counts must plateau at the declared cache policy. After leaving Stay, zero owned RAFs, zero owned observers/listeners and no scene roots remain. Browser heap may retain internal caches, so record the trend and retained object paths rather than claiming every byte must return instantly to baseline.
PART XXII — DATA / CONTENT ARCHITECTURE
22.1 Single factual source with Stay-specific presentation
Preserve src/data/content.ts as the authority for owner, contacts, achievements and current direction, and src/data/projects.ts for its existing four records. New Stay narrative presentation belongs in src/data/stay/*.ts. Do not append SNRLED to an array consumed blindly by Quick and thereby alter its layout. Add src/data/stay/snrled.ts as the single SNRLED record, then compose the Stay project catalog from the four existing records plus SNRLED in explicit order. A future shared catalog migration is outside this scope.
Stay-specific copy is keyed by route and beat ID. Reused factual details reference project/achievement IDs rather than copying full facts into geometry data. The route narration can select or contextualize facts, but there is exactly one status field and evidence record for each project. Shared original narrative strings remain available to Quick unchanged. Explicit safe editorial revisions in the route chapters override inaccurate or overconfident imported wording only within Stay.
Do not bundle the Codex, POS, raw conversation extracts, private source notes or appearance/marriage stories. The public data build uses an allowlist of public records; filtering a private record in a client component is too late. Keep internal provenance in a private authoring ledger outside imported production modules; public evidence labels may identify the type of evidence without revealing private document text.
22.2 Data contracts

StayRoute | id, path, ordinal, label, environment, worldOrigin, beatIds, exitIds, assetIds, posterUrls | Unique ID/path; all references resolve; no forced-next field
NarrativeBeat | id, routeId, scoreStart, scoreEnd, heading, paragraphs, semantics, textMode, sourceRefs | Contiguous ordered score intervals; public copy nonempty; one h1

Public factual invariants: the five Work projects are Study Planner, IGCSE Past Paper Logger & Analytics, MUN Club, SNRLED Parties and Project AURA. STRAT MUN remains an actual conference in Beyond Code / Quick. The desire model is personal philosophy. Leadership over-functioning is a potential risk, not a fabricated incident. AURA is ongoing self-understanding, not a diagnosis or scientific personality model.
Publication readiness is separate from engineering completeness. The site may function with truthful missing-contact and missing-scan states, but it is not ready to serve as a working contact destination until the user supplies real channels. Do not hold all engineering hostage to optional evidence, and do not falsely claim those gaps were resolved. Report them in the release handoff.

src/sections/stay/world/ScrollController.ts | Create | DOM measurement and piecewise route score

public/models/stay/v1/; public/assets/stay/v1/ | Future asset task | Validated GLBs, scene/asset manifests and route posters
tests/stay/; scripts/qa/stay/ | Create during implementation | Evaluator/state tests and bounded browser QA scripts

23.2 Interfaces and lifecycle without a framework explosion
Scene adapter contract: create(context, sceneManifest) returns {evaluate(p, frame), setQuality(tier), getAnchor(id), dispose()}. Context supplies the shared scene parent, AssetStore, light rig, generation and immutable configuration. evaluate performs no network requests, no React setState and no layout reads; it samples clips and transforms only. It must be safe to call twice with the same p and time. dispose is idempotent.
WorldRuntime contract: attachRoute(routeId,generation), detachRoute(generation), setProgress(p), setViewport(width,height,dpr), setMotionMode(mode), requestRender(reason), dispose(). Only attachRoute may change active route ownership. AssetStore returns retained handles, never raw shared resources whose ownership is unclear. The runtime reports readiness and failure through callbacks; content components do not poll renderer internals.
SceneBoundary registers the route’s content root and data-beat attributes in an effect, then unregisters using the same generation. Server content remains passed as children through the client provider rather than imported by it. Per-frame DOM references are cached once; route disposal clears them. Reading-mode CSS is always available without JavaScript.
Preserve MotionScope’s useful cleanup discipline but extend it only where needed for ResizeObserver, abort controllers and custom disposers. Do not create generic abstract scene factories for one-off visuals. Ten short scene adapters plus shared camera/asset/scroll ownership are easier to verify than a monolithic component or a configurable animation language with its own runtime compiler.
Retirement plan: after each route passes visual/content parity and its new fallback tests, remove that route’s old per-page GardenScene/controller wiring. Keep legacy editorial StayAwhile.tsx for old anchors until compatibility tests pass. Do not delete the immutable Claude import, design references, POC, Quick assets or motion-lab studies. No .dc.html evaluator, eval/new Function or support.js import may appear in the production dependency graph.
PART XXIV — IMPLEMENTATION ORDER
24.1 Phase gates and protected surfaces
The Blender production gate precedes web Phase 1. The phase list below is the web implementation sequence requested by the brief; Phase 2 integrates assets already produced under Part I. A scene with missing prerequisites stays at its static fallback and does not pass its 3D exit criterion. Sol must not quietly fabricate a replacement or claim asset production was completed.
Protected set P applies to every phase: src/sections/entry/**; src/sections/quick-discovery/** including existing uncommitted changes; src/app/(entry)/**; src/app/(quick-discovery)/**; design/references/**; public/__design-ref/**; claude-stay-import/**; scripts/blender/quick-discovery/**; public/models/quick-discovery/**; motion-lab studies; existing POC source/assets; package.json and lockfile unless a documented compatibility fix is separately necessary. Root typography, motion and color tokens remain unchanged; Stay overrides are scoped. This implementation does not deploy the site.
Before each phase, inspect git status and the relevant diff, read the installed Next guide for any changed framework API, and record the asset manifest version. After each phase run the narrow checks below. Run the full typecheck/lint/build suite at architectural checkpoints and the final integration gate; do not claim a suite ran merely because an earlier task reported it. No npm test script currently exists, so name the actual harness and command used for new tests.

PART XXV — TESTING / ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA
25.1 Test protocol and completion evidence
The following explicit cases are required implementation tests, not tests already executed for this document. Run against the production out/ static export served over HTTP. Default desktop is 1440×1000, default mobile 390×844. Use fresh and warm cache where specified. Record browser/device, result, screenshot or trace path, console output and defect ID. Do not mark a failure as passed because a fallback hid it; record whether the fallback was the expected result.
Automated checks cover route reachability, semantic structure, deterministic evaluators, state transitions, asset validation and cleanup. Human visual checks judge whether each environment communicates the intended idea. Screen-reader checks use NVDA with a Chromium browser and at least one VoiceOver/Safari pass on an available real Apple device before public release; if unavailable, report that coverage gap rather than implying a pass. No production personal data is required for any test.

25.3 Route visual review rubric
For each route assess all twelve dimensions: composition, depth, typography, motion, camera, environment, interaction, transition, readability, spatial continuity, mobile and reduced motion. A route fails if the scene can be replaced by a generic background without changing how the idea is communicated. The following route-specific evidence determines the assessment.
Route | Spatial evidence | Critical checks
Garden | Low foreground → gate → path → stone gives depth; title settles before map choice; 3D failure still offers every place. | Fronds never cover central text; low camera becomes human-height; map is available at first paint.
Person | Bench and roots reveal relationship through framing; alternating clear sides support the five themes. | Paragraphs remain full opacity; no personal avatar, trophy or sensitive visual cue.
Builder | Eight physical stages demonstrate dependency and structure; camera enters only when the frame is seated. | Rapid/reverse scroll preserves geometry; bench has five distinct artifacts; technical gap readable.
Thinker | Water, shore stones and tea deck provide calm negative space; stillness exceeds motion during reading. | Philosophy label visible, no mirror/reflection legibility issue; mobile list is readable.
Leader | Three paths and five responsibility markers express coordination, not a lone heroic person. | MUN evidence leads; House stays secondary; shadow copy remains a potential risk.
Stories | Four bays reveal in sequence, lamps remain behind and respond to deliberate attention. | No card grid, no private teaser, no forced disclosure; touch and keyboard equal access.
Work | Pavilion depth and alternating stations communicate an archive you move through; each artifact explains a rule. | Five projects only; readable diagrams; archive status on SNRLED; case studies actually open.
Future | Canopy clears, path widens, marker stays distant and negative space grows. | Type becomes larger without overclaiming achievement; final stillness and explicit exits.
AURA | Garden materials persist into frame, documents, connections and spine; strongest transformation has readable holds. | No private paper textures; model remains revisable; mobile frontal version retains narrative.
Contact | Near-empty space, one stone, no particles; contact channels dominate. | Calm static end; no fake links/form; real native exits and honest missing values.

PART XXVI — FAILURE MODES
26.1 Failure modes and recovery matrix
Failure | Detection | Recovery | Forbidden shortcut
F01 GLB 404/corrupt | Asset fetch/parse/manifest mismatch | Keep poster and all DOM; release partial resources; expose quiet retry | Never block map or fabricate primitive substitute
F02 Slow asset | No first usable frame by 2.5s | Finish route on poster; late attach only for current generation | Do not hold a loading veil over content
F03 No WebGL | Context creation throws or unsupported | Skip runtime allocation; static garden illustration and HTML | No browser upgrade wall
F04 Weak hardware | Three unhealthy sample windows | Downgrade one tier at hold; keep content and silhouette | No endless oscillation
F05 Reduced motion | OS or local setting changes | Cancel decorative travel/ambient; ordinary document flow | No opacity-zero text or lingering canvas loop
F06 JS delayed/disabled | No enhancement readiness | SSR content, native links/details and route poster | No JavaScript-only route navigation
F07 Direct route/hash | No existing provider state | Initialize local scene/target from actual URL | No Garden prerequisite
F08 Rapid scroll | Score delta >.18/frame | Sample destination pose, clips and text together | No queued intermediate timers
F09 Navigate mid-animation | New route intent | Invalidate generation, cancel replay and old transition | No stale attachment or double push
F10 Back/Forward | popstate / committed pathname differs | History wins; sample restored scroll and release old scene | No compensating route push
F11 Refresh | Fresh document | Independent route HTML, local poster, correct hash | No module-global pendingArrival dependence
F12 Map during transition | Open map command | Show plan with valid real links; replace/cancel intent safely | No modal stack
F13 Context loss | webglcontextlost | Stop frames and display poster; one explicit retry path | No automatic infinite restore loop
F14 Font/image layout change | fonts.ready / ResizeObserver | Remeasure while preserving current section anchor | No reset to top or division by zero
F15 Storage denied | Read/write exception | Use in-memory visited state; routes unaffected | No uncaught exception
F16 Missing evidence | Null/placeholder/broken image | Accurate pending caption and accessible title | No invented certificate/photo
F17 Contact absent | contactHref returns null | Show Not added yet as text | No href=# or pretend delivery action
F18 Export axis/name drift | Bounds/clip/node validation fails | Fail asset integration gate; return to Blender source | No arbitrary runtime rotations to hide drift
F19 Shared resource disposed | Refcount/test detects use after release | Fix owner accounting; restore scene from valid asset | No route-specific disposal of shared texture
F20 Incomplete public story | Missing approved account | Exclude optional story from public data | Do not fill with psychological interpretation

26.2 Known engineering risks
The highest risks are persistent renderer ownership during App Router transitions, stale asynchronous loads, geometry/DOM divergence after reflow, route asset budgets, and content drift between shared records and imported prose. The architecture isolates each: provider owns lifetime, generation tokens gate attachment, measured beat anchors drive both text and camera, independent assets control loading, and an explicit public catalog selects evidence.
Three.js is 0.186.0 while @types/three is 0.185.4 in the declared package file. Do not upgrade dependencies automatically in this documentation task. During implementation, first check actual types used; if the mismatch causes a real type failure, make the smallest compatibility correction with a documented reason and run protected-route regressions. No added framework is justified simply because it is familiar.
Current static export prefetch behavior requires production preview testing. A working dev server does not prove exported nested route requests work on the target host. Keep real anchors and a normal navigation fallback. Do not change hosting configuration or deploy as part of this specification or an unrequested implementation verification pass.
Content risk is distinct from technical risk. The existence of a polished placeholder does not prove an achievement scan was supplied, and an existing import’s prose is not automatically safe to publish. The corrected public text in this document deliberately removes unsupported causal leadership claims and sensitive teasers. Preserve those decisions through implementation, debugging and optimization.
PART XXVII — SOL HIGH HANDOFF
HOW SOL HIGH SHOULD IMPLEMENT THIS DOCUMENT
Read the entire document before editing. Do not start after reading only Part I or a single route. Treat the current user brief, factual corrections and protected surfaces as binding. Use this specification for the new Stay visual and engineering decisions; do not substitute a generic portfolio template or make major creative choices from scratch.
First confirm Part I asset prerequisites. Inspect the actual manifests, node names, clip names and screenshots; an asset directory existing is not evidence that its contents meet the contract. If a required model is absent or invalid, report that exact asset ID and continue only independent work that does not pretend the missing asset exists. Do not create runtime primitive substitutes, convert a 3D scene to video, or silently repurpose the Quick world.
Follow the real-route architecture exactly. Keep ten route pages and one Stay-owned renderer, with content rendered as HTML. Build shared infrastructure before route adapters. Implement one phase at a time, verify its exit gate, and keep the protected set untouched. Native scroll explores a route; navigation, map and explicit links choose another. Never turn destinations into sequential sections of a single endless page.
Use the route timelines, exact content blocks, material palette, camera knots and text modes. Technical values throughout are DESIGN DECISIONS, so follow them unless a measured failure requires adjustment. If you need to change an art-directed value, record the failed criterion, before/after evidence and smallest correction. Do not rewrite established facts or remove a narrative beat merely to make implementation easier.
Keep state machines deterministic and cancellable. Avoid repeated setTimeout chains for reversible structural animation. If p jumps, evaluate the final scene in the same frame. Browser history, keyboard access and reduced motion take precedence over decorative travel. A 3D problem must degrade to a readable scene, not a broken page.
Do not add libraries unnecessarily. The project already has Next, React, Motion and Three. Read installed Next docs before framework code. Keep route-scoped styles, typed data, clear module ownership and idempotent disposal. Do not replace successful systems without evidence, and do not import the Claude runtime or expose private research material.
Sol’s completion report must include changed files, exact asset manifest version, routes completed, commands actually run with results, the test ledger status, desktop/mobile/reduced screenshots, measured budgets and remaining source gaps. Do not claim deployment, device coverage, real contact details or certificate evidence unless actually supplied and verified. Hand Luna a concrete implementation and reproducible failures, not a request to finish unresolved architecture.
Sol stopping and escalation criteria
Situation | Required response
Missing personal fact | Use specified honest fallback; mark CONTENT GAP; do not invent
Missing required GLB/clip | Block that scene’s 3D gate; identify ID/source; preserve readable route
Performance budget missed | Measure bottleneck; simplify within specified tiers; record visual parity
Ambiguous source conflict | Current user correction wins; retain provenance note internally
Unrelated dirty files | Preserve them; do not reset/format/commit unrelated changes
Asset geometry prevents camera path | Return exact intersection/pose to asset task; do not improvise a different route
Protected route regression | Stop integration gate and repair within Stay boundary
Feature works only in dev | Treat as failed until static export preview passes

PART XXVIII — LUNA MAX HANDOFF
LUNA MAX DEBUGGING AND VERIFICATION HANDOFF
Begin with the implemented production build, this specification and Sol’s change/test report. Read the known gaps before classifying a truthful placeholder as a defect. Verify actual behavior independently; do not accept passed labels without commands, screenshots or traces. The test ledger in Part XXV is the required case inventory, with route-specific expected results in Parts V–XIV.
Run correctness checks in this order: route/static-export reachability; content invariants; no-JS and model-failure fallbacks; keyboard/dialog focus; state-machine interruption/history; deterministic animation boundaries; mobile/reflow; performance and memory; protected landing/Quick comparison. This catches structural failures before visual polish obscures them.
For animation faults, log routeId, generation, p, beatId, clip time, active asset IDs and transition state at the failing frame. Reproduce with direct load, reverse scroll and a large jump. Look for two controllers writing the same transform, world-origin applied twice, exporter axis conversion applied twice, mixer wrap at final time, or a stale asset promise. Fix the owner of the error rather than adding a timer to conceal it.
For routing faults, record URL and pathname commit time separately from scene readiness. Verify only one push per intent, modified clicks remain native and Back cancels the decorative itinerary. For focus faults, record activeElement before open, during containment and after close/commit. For memory faults, compare owned handles and retained roots after repeated cycles; a browser cache is not automatically a leak, but a retained route controller with listeners is.
Visual verification must compare all twelve rubric dimensions per route. A wrong camera silhouette, repeated generic panel layout, unreadable graph or missing reading hold is a functional design defect. Conversely, do not redesign typography or narrative because of personal taste. Repairs should restore the specified experience and preserve content boundaries.
Classify P0 as content/privacy exposure, route inaccessibility or destructive navigation failure; P1 as trapped focus, unreadable essential content, repeated crashes, wrong factual claims or broken primary scene; P2 as material visual/timing/mobile defects; P3 as minor polish. P0/P1 block handoff. Record any remaining P2 with exact affected condition and why it remains. No silent “mostly passed” status.
Luna’s handoff to Astra must include the final commit/diff identifier where available, all explicit test outcomes, unresolved defects, screenshots at canonical poses, production command results, frame/transfer metrics and twenty-cycle cleanup evidence. Do not claim real-device Safari/VoiceOver coverage if that hardware was unavailable. The optimization stage begins after functional verification, not as a substitute for it.
Debug evidence packet
• One row per test: case ID, environment, steps, expected, observed, pass/fail, evidence file and defect reference.
• A contact sheet per route at five canonical scores plus mobile and reduced equivalents.
• Network log for cold Garden and direct Work #snrled, including failed/slow model cases.
• Transition traces for cancellation, latest-intent wins, history restoration and route timeout.
• Owned resource counts across twenty cycles and after leaving Stay.
• Protected-file hash comparison and explanation of any authorized exception.
PART XXIX — ASTRA XHIGH HANDOFF
ASTRA XHIGH FINAL OPTIMIZATION HANDOFF
Start only after Luna verifies functionality. The objective is the same visual and narrative experience with less work, clearer ownership and lower technical debt. Optimization does not authorize a redesign, replacement copy, removal of environmental depth, new route architecture or changes to the approved landing/Quick.
Establish a reproducible baseline: production build, named device/browser, five fixed camera scores per route, screenshots, transfer sizes, frame time, draw calls, triangles, texture estimates and repeated-navigation resource counts. Make one meaningful optimization at a time and compare against that baseline. Reject a smaller bundle if it shifts reading holds, hides evidence or destroys spatial continuity.
Inspect duplicate code and unnecessary components first. Share pure interpolation, cleanup and asset accounting; do not merge ten meaningful scene adapters into one conditional monolith. Remove dead old Stay controller imports only after all references and legacy anchor behavior are accounted for. Remove unused CSS and animation names only with a production bundle/diff check.
Inspect unnecessary React renders, per-frame allocation, repeated DOM querying, layout reads mixed with writes, redundant mixer evaluation, offscreen animation, duplicate GLB fetches, duplicate materials, unbounded caches and stale listeners. Cache stable node references and reuse vectors. Pause inactive systems. Instance repeated props and merge only static meshes that share materials; do not merge away animation joints or interaction anchors.
Review texture and mesh optimization against the original manifests. Re-export from Blender when geometry changes; preserve source traceability and all required node/clip names. Compression needs a measured download/decode gain and no fallback regression. Do not introduce a new dependency merely to save a small local helper. If type quality is weak, strengthen unions and ownership types instead of using broad any casts.
Check naming, module boundaries, disposal documentation, event options, abort handling, CSS scoping and literal unit conventions. A clean console and a passing typecheck do not establish resource correctness; repeat Luna’s lifecycle cases after any cache or renderer change. Preserve privacy allowlists and factual source selection when deduplicating data.
Final acceptance requires unchanged intended screenshots within documented antialias/render tolerance, all P0/P1 tests still passing, no new unexplained performance regression, and a readable final diff. Report before/after measurements and why each optimization is safe. If a necessary correctness/performance fix visibly changes a scene, identify it explicitly with evidence rather than presenting it as an invisible cleanup.

No route is complete if its environment is merely a background behind the same interface. Work must communicate a workshop, Stories exploration, Future openness, AURA a meaningful climax and Contact calm. The acceptance question is whether spatial design and motion communicate the intended idea. “Looks pretty” is not an adequate substitute.
Engineering signoff and content publication signoff are separate records. The former requires all functional, visual, accessibility and performance gates. The latter additionally confirms real contact channels if contact functionality is promised, the truthfulness of evidence placeholders, and the exclusion of private material. Record unresolved gaps plainly; do not erase them to make a completion report cleaner.

C.1 State ownership and invariants
Exactly one writer owns each animated property: camera position belongs to the camera/transition arbiter; asset root placement belongs to the scene adapter; child transforms belong to the assigned clip or ambient channel; body visibility belongs to semantic HTML, with enhancement layered above it. A map-open camera lift suspends the narrative camera, but does not overwrite its saved score. A route commit cancels all outgoing demo timers before registering incoming nodes.
Every state transition can be interrupted. Cancelled fetches may still resolve; generation checks reject stale results before attachment. Hidden-tab time does not fast-forward a long animation queue. A reverse scroll samples the same deterministic pose; it does not attempt to reverse a series of previously fired callbacks. The public reading experience never waits for an animation-end event to expose a link or paragraph.
Route | Ordered narrative state sequence | Beat count
garden | Stay Awhile → Scroll to enter the garden → A place, rather than a page. → Nine places → Choose a path → Stay as long as you like | 6
person | The Person → I am ambitious, and I would rather say so plainly. → Desire → Freedom → Capability → Experience → People → What would it take to become someone I could genuinely admire? | 8
builder | The Builder → Ground rules → When rules meet → Build and revise → A structure that holds → Five things on the bench → Artifacts → I have built real things. I am not yet the engineer I intend to become. | 8
thinker | The Thinker → We are animals that want things badly enough to invent them. → Money matters because it buys choice. → Failing is not the lesson. The return is. → I could build a life nothing could reach. I don’t want it. → Ship it imperfect. Keep the debt on the books. → Keep the question open | 7

APPENDIX D — TYPOGRAPHY ANIMATION TABLE
Role | Entrance / hold / exit contract | Placement and accessibility
TH · Major heading | ≤420ms line mask; 0→1 opacity only after enhancement; settle within first movement segment; no auto exit while visible | One h1 or major beat heading; original Unicode text in DOM; decorative split spans aria-hidden with an unsplit accessible name
TS · Subheading | ≤360ms; translate ≤16px; reveal as a line/phrase, never letter-by-letter across a long heading | Supporting h2/h3 structure; route beat coordinates are desktop art direction, mobile returns to flow
TP · Paragraph | ≤280ms and ≤12px only for short first entrance; fully opaque reading hold; no scroll-linked dimming | Real paragraph; measure height before score bands; ≤38ch regular narrative (≤55ch evidence), full safe width mobile
TL · Label | ≤180ms opacity; no spatial drift | Short project/category label; do not encode status only through color
TN · Navigation | ≤160ms underline/color response; instant focus ring | Real anchor; 48px touch area; labels remain visible at all times
TM · Map | Instant semantic state; selected marker outline ≤180ms | Visible destination names plus list; keyboard works without animated trace
TE · Evidence | ≤180ms disclosure fade, natural layout expansion | FACT / OBSERVATION / PATTERN / HYPOTHESIS / UNKNOWN label retained in text
TQ · Quiet closing | ≤360ms once; stable final hold until user leaves | Contact and closing copy never disappears at p=1

Complete lookup rule: each route’s major beat headings use TH, supporting structural headings use TS and the final closing invitation uses TQ. Every associated public body uses TP; project/category captions use TL; all outgoing links use TN. Evidence blocks override to TE and map labels to TM. Parts V–XIV supply exact copy, score intervals and desktop positions for every major block. This rule is exhaustive for those blocks and avoids an unspecified default animation.
Route | Major blocks covered | Mobile / reduced-motion override
garden | 6 heading/body pairs plus controls; see Part V | Natural flow; max-width and role scale from Part XVII. All text visible and motion-free under reduced preference.
person | 8 heading/body pairs plus controls; see Part VI | Natural flow; max-width and role scale from Part XVII. All text visible and motion-free under reduced preference.
builder | 8 heading/body pairs plus controls; see Part VII | Natural flow; max-width and role scale from Part XVII. All text visible and motion-free under reduced preference.
thinker | 7 heading/body pairs plus controls; see Part VIII | Natural flow; max-width and role scale from Part XVII. All text visible and motion-free under reduced preference.
leader | 6 heading/body pairs plus controls; see Part IX | Natural flow; max-width and role scale from Part XVII. All text visible and motion-free under reduced preference.
stories | 6 heading/body pairs plus controls; see Part X | Natural flow; max-width and role scale from Part XVII. All text visible and motion-free under reduced preference.
work | 7 heading/body pairs plus controls; see Part XI | Natural flow; max-width and role scale from Part XVII. All text visible and motion-free under reduced preference.

APPENDIX E — INTERACTION TABLE
Control / input | Result | Cancel / fallback
Map button / M shortcut | Open accessible map only outside editable fields and active dialogs | Escape closes; focus returns; native dialog fallback list
Destination anchor | Native href plus optional intercepted same-origin transition | Modified click/new tab unchanged; no JS still navigates
Scroll / keyboard page movement | Advance measured narrative within current route | No page change; rapid movement settles destination pose
Back / Forward | Restore browser route and saved narrative anchor | Never replay entry sequence or push extra history
Story lantern or list button | Open corresponding public story panel | Close/Escape returns focus; all public stories available in HTML
Work station / deep link | Focus selected case; reveal factual details and demo controls | Stable #study-planner/#past-paper-logger/#mun-club/#snrled/#project-aura; source links only if verified
Demo Next / Previous / Reset | Deterministic local illustrative state | Leaving station cancels timers; no real backend request
Certificate button | Open labeled scan placeholder or supplied evidence | Truthful availability label; keyboard Close and Escape
Reading-mode toggle | Switch to poster and ordinary content while preserving anchor | Preference failure stays local; content unaffected
Fine pointer near scenery | Small bounded local visual offset only | Zero on blur, touch, reduced preference and map open
Contact channel | Open verified channel when present | Missing channel renders unavailable text, never a placeholder href
Quick Discovery link | Navigate to /quick/ through existing route contract | Dispose Stay-owned runtime; preserve Quick implementation

APPENDIX F — PERFORMANCE BUDGETS
Measure | Desktop | Mobile | Verification
First route HTML/CSS/Stay JS | ≤250 KiB gzip incremental over shared app | ≤220 KiB gzip | Measure transfer; do not count browser cache as zero-cost first visit
Shared garden kit first use | ≤1.8 MiB compressed transfer | ≤700 KiB | Only visible shared assets; G01 world floor may use low tier initially
Active route unique GLBs | ≤2.4 MiB | ≤900 KiB | Work may approach cap; individual asset caps are upper bounds
Initial visible poster | ≤180 KiB | ≤110 KiB | Responsive source, not both downloaded
Total first route 3D transfer | ≤4.5 MiB including kit and route | ≤1.8 MiB | No other destination bundle before intent
Visible triangles | ≤160,000 | ≤55,000 | Count instances and any shadow pass
Visible draw calls | ≤90 baseline / 110 with optional shadow | ≤45 | Renderer.info.render.calls at worst frame
Texture GPU allocation | ≤64 MiB estimated | ≤24 MiB estimated | Include mipmaps, render targets and duplicate uploads
Total GPU residency estimate | ≤128 MiB | ≤64 MiB | Estimate buffers and textures; GPU memory is not directly portable
Active animated transform nodes | ≤80 | ≤30 | Static objects do not need animation tracks
Particles | 48 garden; 8 thinker; 0 contact | 8 garden; 2 thinker; 0 contact | Instanced; drop first on downgrade
Frame time after warm-up | p95 ≤20 ms at 1440×900 | p95 ≤33 ms at 390×844 | 60-second trace on named hardware, foreground, no devtools recording overlap
Layout shift | CLS ≤0.1 | CLS ≤0.1 | Fonts/images sized; case-study click expansion excluded only as expected input shift
Interaction latency | INP target ≤200 ms | INP target ≤200 ms | Field metric requires traffic; use lab interaction traces as proxy
Main-thread stalls | No animation-caused task >50 ms repeatedly | Same | Decode/parse may need chunking and poster-first reveal

Budgets are prospective acceptance limits, not achieved measurements. Record device model, OS, browser/version, viewport, DPR cap, network conditions, production build identity and sample duration with every result. A desktop emulation screenshot is not a physical mobile GPU test. Do not report INP as a measured field percentile before representative traffic exists.
APPENDIX G — RESPONSIVE BEHAVIOR MATRIX
Route | Required mobile composition
Stay Awhile | Use a centered rail, camera height 1.2→1.55 m, fronds confined to outer 12% of the viewport and title width ≤11ch. The map control is visible from first paint.
The Person | Camera lateral x is multiplied by .35; use one readable column at 20 px horizontal padding. Bench and roots frame the upper visual region, never the body copy.
The Builder | Move camera to (0,1.7,z+1.5), reduce side travel to 35%; bench occupies top 40%, copy flows below. Preserve all five project labels without shrinking.
The Thinker | Hold camera at (1.2,1.6,z+1), remove pointer offsets and waves in low tier. Body uses 20–22 px Garamond; freedom labels are a wrapping semantic list, never tiny stone textures.
The Leader | Use centered camera and shallow convergence; process labels stay in DOM. At 320 px wrap the five responsibilities vertically and preserve their order.
Stories | Reduce side bends by 40%; every lantern has a 48 px HTML Read story button below its title. The story text stays in normal flow and does not require hover.
The Work | Narrow camera aisle at x=0 and FOV 52; station props occupy top 36% while HTML flows underneath. Use explicit Previous/Next station anchor links, no horizontal swipe requirement.
The Future | Preserve a central horizon at 42% viewport height, no roll. Text starts below the sky opening; direction labels wrap at 320 px. Remove far foliage first.
Project AURA | Use a frontal 52-degree camera, movement depth scaled .6 and no orbit. Nine documents retain full labels in a separate ordered list. On low tier use five stage illustrations and the same prose.
Contact | One column, 24 px gaps, 48 px targets. No camera travel after entry, no floating contact text, no hover-only actions.

At 320–479px use a single reading column, 20px normal horizontal padding (16px only when needed for 320px), safe-area-aware fixed controls and a compact numbered map. At 480–767px retain single-column sequencing with a larger scenery region. At 768–1023px allow side composition only when the measured text width remains comfortable. At 1024–1439px use the canonical desktop rail. At 1440px and wider cap text width and increase environment breathing room rather than stretching paragraphs.
Short landscape viewports below 600px height disable sticky full-screen reading holds and return content to ordinary flow. A 200% zoom or large-text setting that causes the same pressure gets the same fallback. Touch and coarse-pointer capability, not a user-agent string, determines hover removal. Neither resizing nor rotating may reset the current story, lose a demo’s approved state or navigate to a different route.
APPENDIX H — ACCESSIBILITY MATRIX
Surface | Semantic / focus contract | Motion-free equivalent
Every route | Landmarks, one h1, ordered headings, skip link, real links, meaningful page title | Complete server HTML and poster
Canvas | Decorative aria-hidden; no keyboard trap or required raycast | No canvas required to understand any claim
Map | Dialog name; initial focus; contained Tab; Escape; invoking focus restore | SVG + native anchor list
Stories | Button expanded state and associated labeled region; safe focus management | All public story content reachable in DOM
Demos | Labeled button controls; concise polite announcement only for explicit steps | Ordered static steps and visible state table
Certificates | Dialog label, scan alt/caption, visible Close, truthful placeholder | Text award statement and unavailable scan caption
Navigation | Current route indicated in text/aria-current; 48px target, visible focus | Direct anchors; no animated prerequisite
Typography | Contrast at least WCAG AA; no image text; 200% resize and 400% reflow checks | No opacity suppression or clipped masks
Contact | Verified accessible channel names; missing channels noninteractive | Same factual availability
Runtime error | Poster retained, links operable, concise status without repeated live announcements | Ordinary content remains the primary surface

Test at least keyboard-only, OS reduced motion, forced colors, large text, 320 CSS-pixel reflow, one desktop screen reader and one mobile screen reader when hardware is available. Document unavailable coverage. Automated accessibility tooling assists but does not prove dialog focus, reading order or motion comfort.

This is a proposed implementation layout. It does not claim these future files already exist. Preserve existing native route entry files. Keep import-only Claude/support files in reference directories. Do not move protected Quick/landing sources into this subsystem or make them depend on the new Stay runtime. Test tooling must be selected from the actual repository/toolchain at implementation time; this specification does not authorize random dependency installation.

Only missing facts or genuine publication decisions belong in this ledger. Camera knots, material recipes, asset structure, movement durations, route architecture and interaction behavior are specified design decisions, not questions returned to the user. The implementation agent should proceed with the defined fallback when a source gap does not block engineering. A private omission must never become a public “coming soon” teaser.
APPENDIX K — KNOWN RISKS AND SOURCE REGISTER
Risk | Early signal | Required containment
Spatial design becomes a card page | Same composition reused on all ten routes | Compare route-specific landmark/camera rubric before polish
Unrealistic small-screen staging | Text overlaps model at 320px or zoom | Flow fallback; route-specific camera and lower asset density
Animation ownership conflict | Jitter after map close or reverse scroll | One property writer; explicit camera arbiter and generation token
Export/runtime disagreement | Missing clip/root or swapped axes | Validate manifests, units and five canonical poses before integration
Accumulating renderer resources | Memory/listeners climb across twenty visits | Reference counts, disposal ledger, bounded route cache and exit audit
Source overclaim | Rewritten copy turns hypothesis into fact | Evidence category survives data schema, UI and review
Misleading archived demo | SNRLED appears live or historical prices are invented | Archived label; illustrative data badge; no backend connection
Protected experience regression | Quick or landing diff appears in Stay work | Protected path hashes and explicit scope review
Fictional proof | Generated certificates/photos imply real evidence | Keep source placeholders truthful; no fabricated imagery
Test report overstatement | Emulation described as physical device coverage | Named environment and explicit untested matrix rows

K.1 Source hierarchy and freshness
Source authority for this specification: the user’s current implementation-document request and explicit factual corrections take priority; the Project AURA Master Codex supplies evidence and epistemic context; current native source and data establish implemented behavior; Claude imports and design studies establish reference intent; external sites supply observed design mechanics only. Proposed numbers in this document are engineering/art-direction decisions unless explicitly identified as measured current repository facts.
Audit date: 22 September 2026. The current worktree, including uncommitted Quick changes, was inspected as supplied. Existing build/test success is not asserted by this document. The document task did not run or alter a production website implementation. Local source hashing provides a before/after check that the audit and authoring did not change the inspected production/reference files.
